# Implementation Notes

## 2026-10-27

- Native internal TestFlight build 149 (1.0.1): Apron links (link.apron.chat and `apron:` links open in the app, with confirmation for new servers, sign-in tokens and unjoined rooms; Copy Link on Apron messages). Upload reached `processing=VALID internal=READY_FOR_BETA_TESTING`.
- Native internal TestFlight build 148 (1.0.1): Telegram (chats, archive, media, avatars, reactions, push), optimistic sending, muted badges, full chat-row redaction, the page-dots pill, and Apron push. Built with stupid-app 0.0.21, which copies SwiftPM resource bundles under SwiftPM's `<Package>_<Target>.bundle` name (0.0.20 silently left them out, so `Bundle.module` would have crashed). The IPA carries `NoFeedSocial_NoFeedSocialCore.bundle` (schema and the gitignored `TelegramAPI.json`) and `en.lproj/Localizable.strings`. The notification extension needed its first App Store profile (`signing setup --kind distribution --bundle-id tech.stupid.StupidSocial.NotificationService`). The app's App Store profile was from 2026-09-02 and lacked push, App Groups, Siri and Communication Notifications; `signing setup` reuses an existing profile, so it was regenerated in the developer portal (Edit → Save) and fetched again. Upload reached `processing=VALID internal=IN_BETA_TESTING`. Then released to external TestFlight: `release external-beta --group ac67336d-4435-4ad7-9c6d-17ef19701dd1 --no-wait` with a short, high-level What to Test covering the changes since the last external build (137): the Chats screen across Apron, Spotify, Instagram and Telegram; Telegram sign-in; Apron push; faster sending with photos. `release status --live` reported external `IN_BETA_TESTING`.
- "Redact names" now covers every Chats row (user report: Telegram group titles showed). Titles were only redacted when they named people (DMs, member-named rooms); every title is now "Redacted", including the Archived Chats row's list, and row avatars show the initial instead of the photo while redaction is on.
- Muted chats show their unread count on a gray badge (product request): `ChatSummary.isMuted` (stored optional, so cached lists from earlier builds decode). Telegram: the dialog's `notify_settings.mute_until` is in the future (`2147483647` for chats muted forever, seen with the probe); Apron: the room is in the connection's `mutedRooms` (level None). Instagram and Spotify don't report a mute the app reads.
- Telegram media, avatars, reactions, sending photos and push (see `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` § Telegram).
  - Probe first: `download` (photo from DC 4, 92 KB in 2 s) and `avatar` (Hypersnap's photo from DC 1 via `auth.exportAuthorization`/`importAuthorization`, from a DC 4 account) validated file downloads and the cross-DC sign-in; `send-photo` validated `upload.saveFilePart` + `messages.sendMedia` (into Saved Messages). `react` failed in Saved Messages with `PREMIUM_ACCOUNT_REQUIRED` (reactions there are Premium tags); testing in a group was blocked by the auto-mode classifier, so reaction toggling follows the schema and isn't live-tested.
  - Images load through a `tgfile:` URL scheme served by a `URLProtocol`, so `CachedAsyncImage`, chat bubbles and the full-screen viewer needed no changes. `URLProtocol` already has a `task` property (renamed `loadTask`), and its client is called through a small `@unchecked Sendable` wrapper.
  - On the simulator: photos, sender avatars (including "min" users through `inputPeerUserFromMessage`), reactions, reply quotes and a video card with its thumbnail rendered in groups; Chats rows show avatars; reading a group in the app took its unread count on Telegram to 0. A photo sent from the composer reached Telegram.
  - Reply quotes said "Loading" for a long time (user report). Telegram never fetched quoted messages outside the page; now they load in one `getMessages` call before the page shows. Apron had the same symptom although the server answers a single-message `history` in about 0.2 s (`apron-web-client.py history --after X --before X --limit 1`, which now prints `_elapsed_s`): the fetches started only after the first display, one task each, after connecting, joining, history and opening at the first unread. They now run in parallel and the first load waits for them.
  - Push: Web Push (token type 10) is what Telegram Web uses (tweb registers `{endpoint, keys, vapid: true}` with Telegram's VAPID key `BHEbKOXt-…KPM` and an empty `secret` unless a passcode is set), and the relay would accept it unchanged, but `account.registerDevice` answers `APP_PUSH_APIKEY_MISSING` for this api_id. my.telegram.org/apps has a Push credentials section with APNs certificate upload (`apps/apns_cert`, production and development PEM). An "Apple Push Notification service SSL (Sandbox & Production)" certificate for `tech.stupid.StupidSocial` was created in the developer portal from a locally generated CSR (key kept outside the repository; certificate valid to 2027-11-26) and uploaded as certificate + key PEM to both slots; token type 1 registration then returned `true`. A tunnel to capture a real Web Push was blocked by the auto-mode classifier.
  - APNs alerts from Telegram are `loc-key`/`loc-args`; without strings iOS would show the key. `scripts/telegram-push-strings.py` writes 190 templates to `Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings` (`{1}` → `%1$@`); `Info.plist` gained `CFBundleDevelopmentRegion = en`. A simulated push (`stupidsim push` with `CHAT_MESSAGE_TEXT`) showed "gramajo @ stupid wallet testflight: Might have to jack that idea", and opening it opened that chat. A real Telegram push needs a device build.
- Optimistic sending for every chat (product request), moved into `ChatViewModel.sendOptimistically`: the message shows at once with a clock beside its time, and the network's copy replaces it. Networks now set `history` instead of `messages`. The first version drew the sending photo with link-preview padding and swapped to Telegram's copy with a reload and a size change (user reports "bigass border", "layout shifts"); the sending photo now uses the media layout, and the confirmed message keeps the picked image file and its aspect ratio, so the swap changes only the time badge (851 pixels between the frames before and after).
- The home pager's page dots sit on a small pill (`UIVisualEffectView`, `.systemMaterial`, 7×5 pt padding) so they stay legible over list content (product request). The thin material was too faint over white rows.
- Added Telegram chats (see `docs/PLAN.md` § Telegram and `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` § Telegram). Telegram has no HTTP API for user accounts and stupid-app rejects binary targets (`Planner.swift`: "binaryTarget … unsupported"), so TDLib was ruled out and the app implements MTProto 2.0 itself. Decisions: chats only (no feed items or push yet); broadcast channels and archived chats are skipped; media shows as labels; the session is device-local (Keychain, not synchronizable); the API id and hash are build-time, from the gitignored `Resources/TelegramAPI.json`, since the repository is public.
  - Probe first: `scripts/telegram-client.py` is a stdlib-only MTProto client (pure-Python AES with an IGE wrapper checked against the FIPS-197 vector, RSA_PAD, DH, Pollard–Brent, schema-driven TL codec, SRP). `handshake` created a real auth key on DC 2 in about 2 s (server fingerprint matched TDLib's RSA key; DH prime matched). The other commands were run live later (below).
  - The TL codec is generic over the official schema (`scripts/telegram-schema.py` writes `Resources/TelegramSchema.tl` from core.telegram.org's JSON, layer 225, deduplicated by constructor id). The MTProto and API schemas both define `message`; encoding by name prefers the API one, and decoding goes by id.
  - Swift matches the probe byte for byte: `TelegramTests` checks AES-IGE, a 2048-bit modpow, SRP (with a fixed secret), and a `messages.sendMessage` encoding against vectors from the probe, plus gzip, flag round trips and message previews. `testLiveHandshake` (run with `TEST_RUNNER_TELEGRAM_LIVE_TESTS=1`) creates an auth key with DC 2 from the simulator, and passed. A modpow takes about 1 s in debug builds (unoptimized Swift); the key exchange runs once per device and SRP once per sign-in.
  - Resources: `NoFeedSocialCore` now has SwiftPM resources (`.process("Resources")`). stupid-app copied resource bundles only as `<Target>.bundle`, while SwiftPM names them `<Package>_<Target>.bundle`, so the bundle never reached the app and `Bundle.module` would have crashed. Fixed in stupid-app (see that repo).
  - `TLSchema` is `@unchecked Sendable` (immutable after init; its parameter tuples aren't inferred `Sendable`). The transport's one-shot start flag is a small class, since `NWConnection` state handlers are `@Sendable`.
  - Badge: `TelegramBadge` PNGs (blue circle, white paper plane), drawn with ImageMagick.
  - Calls time out after 25 s by closing the session, since a socket iOS dropped while suspended can stay silent; the client then reconnects and retries.
  - Live with a real account (probe first, then the app, which are separate Telegram authorizations): the account lives on DC 4, so `auth.sendCode` answered `PHONE_MIGRATE_4` from DC 2 and both clients moved there; sign-in needed the 2FA password, and SRP passed in both. The probe reads the password from `TELEGRAM_USER_PASSWORD` in `.env.local` (the `!` shell has no terminal for a prompt), and the API credentials from `TELEGRAM_APP_ID`/`TELEGRAM_APP_API_HASH`. In the app: the Chats list merged Telegram chats, Saved Messages history loaded, a message sent from the composer reached Telegram (read back with the probe), and a message the probe sent appeared in the open chat within 3 s through a pushed update.
  - `messages.getDialogs` without `folder_id` returns all folders mixed (636 dialogs = 515 in folder 0 + 121 in the Archive), so archived chats were showing in Chats. The main list now passes `folder_id: 0`, and the Archive (`folder_id: 1`) is a separate section behind an Archived Chats row (product request). `dialogs --archived` in the probe lists it.
  - Saved Messages messages carry no `out` flag (seen with the probe: only `peer_id` and `saved_peer_id`, both the user), so they showed as incoming. A message is now the user's own when it has `out` or its sender is the signed-in user.
  - `listen` showed an active account gets constant `updateUserStatus` updates, so the chat no longer refreshes on every update: `TelegramClient.update(_:concerns:)` keeps only message, delete and read updates for the open chat (shapes from the probe, covered by `TelegramTests`).
  - On the simulator: without `TelegramAPI.json`, Settings shows "Telegram, Unavailable" and the Chats list loads as before. A temporary build with a dummy API id (`1`) got "Telegram error 400: API_ID_INVALID" from Send Code. So the Swift key exchange, encrypted session, `invokeWithLayer(initConnection)`, `auth.sendCode` and RPC error path work against Telegram end to end. (Later tested with a real account, above.)

- Changed the notification layout (product choice among four mocked layouts): the title is "<sender> in <room>" (the sender alone in DMs), the subtitle only the server host, and the "mentioned you" / "replied to you" labels are gone. The extension now donates every message as one-to-one from a person named like the title, with no group name or recipients, so iOS's communication layout keeps that title; the person's identifier is still per sender. `privateRooms` left the registration (it only served the reply label); older stores still decode. Verified with the real extension under stupidsim 0.0.0-dev: `ran …: title "shazow in General", subtitle "server.apron.chat", body "Deploy is done @stephancill, see the dashboard"`, a Communication Notification.
- The Notification Service Extension can now be tested on the simulator with a stupidsim development build (0.0.0-dev): `stupidsim push` runs the app's real `.appex` for `mutable-content` payloads and prints what it produced. With the App Group store seeded with a registration's keys and RFC 8291 ciphertext encrypted to them, everything passed: decrypted title, subtitle ("General · mentioned you · server.apron.chat") and plain-text body; the Communication Notification path; the fallback "Apron / New message" for an undecryptable payload; and Reply plus cold-launch open on a serviced banner. Builds before stupidsim commit c6accb5 failed to deliver without `--bundle-id`, because the extension replaces `userInfo` and so drops the payload's `Simulator Target Bundle` key. Fixed there after we reported it, and confirmed without `--bundle-id`.
- The notification subtitle now always ends with the server host (it was only added with two or more servers saved), per product decision.
- Fixed duplicate pushes. Registration could run twice at once for one server (a new connection, and the device token arriving or another connection), and each run fetched its own relay URL before either saved it, so the server kept two or three registrations for the same `push_id` and sent one push to each (eight relay requests for three messages on the local server). `ApronPushService.register` now chains registrations per server, so each waits for the one before and reuses the relay URL it saved. Stale registrations on a server expire only when not renewed (30 days on apron-server-go, 7 on the demo); the two on the local test server were removed with the probe's `push-unregister`. After reinstalling, the local server held one registration and one mention produced one push.
- Device checks through iPhone Mirroring: the test users now have avatars (`me` probe command, `--avatar`), which show in the Chats list and beside their messages in the chat. Opening a DM removed its two delivered notifications while a mention in another room stayed (Debug list). Synthesized horizontal scrolls in iPhone Mirroring once left the home pager stuck halfway between pages with a chat pushed; relaunching recovered it. Not reproduced with real touches.

- Fixed a crash when a notification launched the app: the `nonisolated async` `userNotificationCenter(_:didReceive:)` returned off the main thread, and UIKit aborted in `-[UIApplication _performBlockAfterCATransactionCommitSynchronizes:]` (SIGABRT, found by the stupidsim maintainer). Tapping into a notification therefore only worked while the app was already running. Both delegate methods now use the completion-handler forms and call the handler from a `@MainActor` task. Verified on the simulator with stupidsim 0.4.4 and the app terminated each time, with no new crash reports: `stupidsim notification open --match …` on a banner opened its room; swiping a Notification Center row to the right (iOS 27 rows only nudge on tap; rows come from `ui-tree --springboard`) opened its room; `stupidsim notification reply` sent a reply that came back as "Reply not sent … unknown room" (nonexistent room on purpose).
  On the iPhone, with the app closed, tapping a DM notification from the local test server opened the app into that chat without a crash. iPhone Mirroring can't reach notifications (no Notification Center, and none were forwarded to the Mac), so this tap was done on the phone.
- Added a local test server for device testing. `scripts/apron-local-server.sh` builds `third-party/apron-server-go` and serves it on `0.0.0.0:8090` with state and log in `logs/apron-local/`, email codes written to the log (`--email.sender log`), any origin allowed (native clients send none), and push to the real relay. Probe commands: `email-signup` (proposes an email sign-in, reads the code from the log, approves with `--user-id`/`--name`, keeps the token in `--token-file`) and `room-create` (`room_set`, `--private`, `--title`, `--add` members by `room_join`). The Debug settings screen now lists delivered notifications (title, subtitle, body, thread, category, whether the extension rewrote them), since iPhone Mirroring can't open the phone's Notification Center.
- Device test on the iPhone through iPhone Mirroring against the local server (accounts `stephan` on the phone, `alice` and `bob` driven by the probe), checked in that list and in the relay logs:
  - DM from Alice: "Alice" / "192.168.111.71" / text (the server shows because two servers are saved).
  - Mention in General with Markdown: "Bob" / "General · mentioned you · 192.168.111.71" / "Deploy is done @Stephan, ask @Alice about the dashboard".
  - Reply to the user's message in General: "General · replied to you".
  - Message in a private room ("Team"): woke without a mention (`private` scope), backticks removed.
  - Setting Team to None in its member list stored `room_mutes` for it on the server; Bob's next message there sent no push while a control mention in General did (one relay request). Setting it back to All Messages cleared the mute.
  - The server pushes only after the app's connection closes: a message sent while the app is still in front gets no push.
  - Not checkable through Mirroring: how iOS draws the notification (avatar position, whether the communication layout keeps the subtitle), tapping a notification, and the Reply action. The test accounts have no avatars.
  - Seen in passing: the Chats list shows raw Markdown in previews, and two rooms named General on different servers look the same there.

- Fixed tapping a notification while the feed side was showing: `HomePager.updateUIViewController` ignored programmatic `page` changes, so the router set `page = .chats` but the pager stayed on the feed while the room opened unseen on the Chats side (and paging turned off). It now calls `setViewControllers` and updates the dots when `page` differs from the shown page. Tapping a notification for another room while a chat is open also did nothing, because a showing `navigationDestination(item:)` doesn't swap when its item changes; `ChatsView` now clears the selection and opens the new chat 450 ms later. Verified on the simulator with foreground banners carrying `userInfo.apron`: from the feed side, and from one open room to another. The simulator can't tap lock-screen notifications through synthesized input, so cold launch from a notification is untested there.
- Reworked notification content (product choices recorded in `docs/PLAN.md`): sender title with avatar via Communication Notifications, room/context/server subtitle, plain text with named mentions, and an inline Reply action. Details in `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` § Apron Push. Development profiles were regenerated as "Push Comms" for the app after enabling Communication Notifications and Siri in the portal (`signing setup` hit an HTTP 500 on the extension step; its "Push Group" profile stays valid because its App ID didn't change). stupid-wallet-2 found that iOS's communication layout may not show `subtitle`; if the room line is missing on the device, move it into the body. `ApronPushTests` (17) pass, including reading a store written by the earlier build.
- Simulator checks (`stupidsim push`, which wraps `simctl push`): the Reply action works. Long-pressing a banner in category `apron.message`, typing and tapping Send posted through the app. A reply to a nonexistent room on `server.apron.chat` (so nothing was sent to anyone) came back as a "Reply not sent … unknown room" notification, both with the app suspended (the UI's `ApronService`) and with the app terminated (iOS launched it in the background and the standalone `ApronService` connected). The service extension never runs on the simulator: neither `stupidsim push` nor `simctl push` launched it, even with the app re-signed to carry `aps-environment`. Decryption, layout, avatars and the communication presentation can only be seen on a device.
- The push relay now keeps its APNs provider token in the data center's cache (`caches.default`, key `https://push.stupidtech.net/internal/apns-provider-token/<key id>`, 49 minutes) as well as in memory. Live pushes from `server.apron.chat` were mostly accepted (201), but 2 of 10 got APNs `429`: pushes minutes apart often start a new isolate, each signing a fresh token, and APNs refuses token refreshes more often than every 20 minutes (`TooManyProviderTokenUpdates`). The relay also logs the APNs reason for every refusal now, 429 included. Requests to the cache key's URL reach the Worker, which answers 404, so the cache never serves the token.

## 2026-10-26

- Added Apron push notifications (protocol §4.9), with per-room levels. Design in `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` § Apron Push.
  - **Server support (checked 2026-10-26):** `apron-server-go` offers `relay` and `webpush` with every wake scope and accepts any public https endpoint. `apron-server-cloudflare` (the `server.apron.chat` demo, `apron-cloudflare-demo/9`) offers only `webpush`, wakes for `mentions`, `replies` and `private`, and allows only the hosts in `PUSH_HOSTS` (by default the browsers' push services). The demo operator agreed to allowlist `push.stupidtech.net`. Neither server has per-room wake scopes; room `mute` is the only per-room control, hence the proposed `room_wake` extension.
  - **Why Web Push to a relay:** native apps can't get a browser push subscription, and the demo doesn't offer `relay`. Registering `webpush` everywhere gives one encrypted format (RFC 8291 `aes128gcm`) and one decryption path. The relay seals the device token, environment and the server's VAPID key into the endpoint id, so it needs no database and refuses pushes signed by any other key.
  - **Probe:** `scripts/apron-web-client.py` gained `push-register`, `push-unregister`, `mute [--room R] [--off]`, `push-capture` (a local endpoint that logs headers and decrypts bodies) and `send --mention`. P-256, HKDF and AES-128-GCM are implemented without dependencies and checked against the FIPS-197 AES vector and the RFC 8291 Appendix A example. Not yet run against a live server: starting the local Go server from this session was blocked by the permission check, and `server.apron.chat` refuses endpoints outside its allowlist until `push.stupidtech.net` is added. Run with `go run ./cmd/aprond --store memory --push.allow-insecure --addr 127.0.0.1:8090` in `third-party/apron-server-go`, `push-capture --port 8099`, `push-register ws://127.0.0.1:8090/ws --endpoint http://127.0.0.1:8099/p`, then a mention from another account after the first disconnects.
  - **Relay:** `workers/push-relay` (Worker `stupid-push-relay`, custom domain `push.stupidtech.net`), signing with APNs key `U7W6CW4B57` (team `6JKMV57Y77`; one of the two APNs keys found, checked by posting to a fake device token: APNs keys get `BadDeviceToken`, other keys `InvalidProviderToken`). `bun run push-relay:test` covers registration, delivery to a mocked APNs, refusing other VAPID keys, the wrong audience, expired tokens, forged ids and APNs token rejection. Deployed 2026-10-26 with secrets `RELAY_SECRET` and `APNS_PRIVATE_KEY`. Checked live with a throwaway VAPID key and a fake device token: `/register` 200, an unsigned push 403, a signed push 410 (APNs authenticated the provider token and rejected the fake device token as `BadDeviceToken`; a refused provider token would be 502). Changing `RELAY_SECRET` invalidates every endpoint.
  - **App:** new `NoFeedSocialPush` module and `NoFeedSocialNotificationService` extension (`tech.stupid.StupidSocial.NotificationService`; an extension's bundle ID has to start with the app's, so it keeps the `tech.stupid` prefix). The app adds `aps-environment` and App Group `group.tech.stupid.StupidSocial`; `stupid-app.yml` declares `ICLOUD`, `PUSH_NOTIFICATIONS` and `APP_GROUPS` capabilities and the extension. `server.push` is decoded in `ApronServerInfo`. The APNs environment comes from the embedded profile's `aps-environment` (absent in App Store/TestFlight builds, so production; sandbox on the simulator).
  - **Simulator limits:** `stupid-app run --simulator` strips `aps-environment` from its ad-hoc signature because the simulator refuses to launch a team-less signature carrying it (POSIX 163, reproduced by re-signing with it), so the simulator gets no APNs token. `simctl push` doesn't run Notification Service Extensions either: a push encrypted to keys seeded in the App Group store arrived with the relay's fallback text. Decryption is covered by `ApronPushTests` (RFC 8291 vector) instead, and the token, relay and extension still need a check on a device.
  - **Verified on the simulator:** the permission prompt appears when a push-capable server connects with a token; tapping a foreground notification banner carrying `userInfo.apron` opened that room (`shazow, vrypan`); a private room's member list offers All Messages / None (default All), a public room's Mentions Only / None (default Mentions); setting None on `server.apron.chat` survived a relaunch (the mute came back in the sign-in `status` frames) and was restored to All Messages. `ApronPushTests` (13) and `ApronModelsTests` pass; `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`.
  - **Signing:** the App Group `group.tech.stupid.StupidSocial` ("stupid social") was registered in the developer portal and assigned to App IDs `tech.stupid.StupidSocial` (`PLUT433P89`) and `tech.stupid.StupidSocial.NotificationService` (`YKBJC2VDZU`); the API can enable `APP_GROUPS` but not create or assign a group, so profiles made before that carry an empty group list. `stupid-app signing setup --kind development --profile-name "Push Group"` then made development profiles authorizing `aps-environment: development` and the group (the default and `Push` names hit HTTP 409 duplicate names, since saving App ID capabilities invalidates but keeps old profiles). Installed over Wi-Fi with `stupid-app run --network` on 2026-10-26. On the iPhone the app registered with the relay right after notification permission was granted, and from 2026-10-27 `server.apron.chat` (with `push.stupidtech.net` allowlisted) delivered pushes that APNs accepted and the user saw as notifications. A distribution profile still needs the same `signing setup --kind distribution` before release.

- Chat reaction changes no longer jump. `applyReactionTotals` now reconfigures changed bubbles inside a 0.3 s ease-out animation that also keeps the list pinned to the bottom (`applyKeepingBottom(_:animated:)`), so a growing bubble moves the list up smoothly instead of snapping. It isn't used before the initial scroll or while anchored to the unread divider. Reaction totals that arrive while a message's context menu is showing wait until the menu finishes dismissing (`willDisplayContextMenu` / `willEndContextMenuInteraction`), so the bubble doesn't grow under the dismissal preview.
- Added reactions to Instagram chats. Mutation `IGDirectReactionSendMutation` (root `xig_direct_reaction_send_with_slide_messaging_response`, doc ID from the thread page's scripts) takes `{input: {emoji, item_id: "", message_id, reaction_status: "created" | "deleted", thread_id: <thread_fbid>}}` and returns `slide_message.reactions`. Instagram keeps one reaction per person per message: a new emoji replaces the previous one. It stores emoji without the variation selector (❤️ comes back as ❤), and deleting only works with the stored form ("❤️" left the reaction in place, "❤" removed it). Probe: `direct-react <thread_fbid> <message_id> <emoji> [--remove]`, validated live on the account's own message (add, replace, remove; restored to no reactions).
  - Native: `InstagramClient.setDirectReaction` and `InstagramChatViewModel` override `yourEmojis`/`toggleReaction`; `canReact` turns on once the thread loads. Toggling your current emoji removes it (sending the stored form), any other emoji replaces it. The change shows right away, is then replaced by the reactions Instagram returns, and is put back if the request fails. Stored emoji map to the matching quick reaction when they differ only by U+FE0F (`displayEmoji`), for grouping, your-reaction checks and reactor lists. Verified on the simulator: long-press → ❤️ showed a "❤️ 1" chip and the thread had the reaction; tapping the chip removed it on Instagram too.
- Spotify and Instagram chats now open with content instead of empty. Both used to show a blank chat for 2–3 seconds until their first request returned, and Spotify's title flashed the partner's username (often a random ID) because `connect()` set it from `user(username:)` before the profile loaded. A new `ChatSnapshotCache` (Caches/`chat-snapshots`, one JSON file per chat, keyed `spotify:<chatId>` / `instagram:<threadFbid>`) keeps each chat's newest 50 raw messages plus what renders them: Spotify profiles, track and share metadata, partner and title; Instagram people, viewer id, legacy thread id and read receipt. The view models restore it in `init` and save it after the first load and on `disconnect()`. Paging state comes only from the live load (`hasLoadedHistory`, which replaces the old `loaded.isEmpty` check that a restored snapshot would have defeated). Spotify keeps the title it opened with until the partner's profile name arrives, and refreshes cached profiles once per visit. Verified by screen recording on the simulator: after one visit, reopening a Spotify chat and an Instagram thread shows the messages from the first frame of the push, the title doesn't change, and nothing shifts when the live data merges.
- Fixed the Apron chat jumping on open in rooms with uploaded images. A chat first renders its cached messages, before the connection is up, and image embeds only load from the server's `ext.media` origins, which came only from the live session. Cached uploads on `media.apron.chat` therefore rendered as small cards, then grew into tall images once the session arrived, pushing the list down before it re-pinned to the bottom. `ApronMessageCache.Entry` now also stores the server's media origins (optional, so older caches still decode), and `ApronChatViewModel` uses them until the session is up. Verified by screen recording on the simulator: after one open rewrites the cache, the next open shows the image from the first frame with no shift.
- Moved Instagram Direct to the web inbox's GraphQL operations and added Instagram chats. For the diagnose account, `GET /api/v1/direct_v2/inbox/` answers HTTP 404 with a logged-out page even though `session-check` and `/direct/inbox/` show a valid session: the web inbox now runs on "IGD Off-Msys" Relay operations. The `direct_v2` REST path (feed DMs, and the unvalidated `direct-thread`/`direct-seen`/`direct-send-text` probe commands) is replaced, with no REST fallback.
  - **Operations (all `POST /api/graphql` with `fb_api_req_friendly_name` and `X-Root-Field-Name`; `/graphql/query` returns a null root):** `PolarisDirectInboxMobileQuery` (root `get_slide_mailbox_for_iris_subscription`, variable `device_id_for_iris_subscription`), `IGDThreadListOffMsysPaginationQuery` (`fetch__SlideMailbox`; `id` = mailbox id, `count`, `cursor`, `folder: "INBOX"`), `IGDThreadDetailQuery` (`get_slide_thread_nullable`; `thread_fbid`, `min_uq_seq_id: null`), `IGDMessageListOffMsysQuery` (`fetch__SlideThread`; `id` = thread fbid, `after` = previous `slide_messages.page_info.end_cursor`), `useIGDMarkThreadAsReadMutation` (`xig_direct_item_seen_mutation_with_slide_messaging_response`; `{data: {item_id: "", message_id}, metadata: {ig_thread_igid: <legacy thread_id>}}`) and `IGDirectTextSendMutation` (`xig_direct_text_send_with_slide_messaging_response`; `ig_thread_igid` = thread fbid, `offline_threading_id` = ms << 22 | 22 random bits, `text.sensitive_string_value`, optional `reply_to_message_id`, other fields null). Relay provided variables (`__relay_internal__pv__…`) are sent with the values the web computes (professional GK false, pinned threads true, max unread 5, thread list actions true, chat themes false, initial page 20).
  - **Device id:** the inbox query returns a null mailbox unless `device_id_for_iris_subscription` is the page config `IGDMqttWebDeviceID.clientId` from a Direct page (not `ig_did`).
  - **Doc IDs:** `/direct/inbox/` page scripts carry only the inbox, inbox pagination and thread detail queries; the message list, send and read operations are in the scripts of a real thread page `/direct/t/<thread_fbid>/` (`/direct/t/0/` serves the inbox bundle set). `InstagramClient` loads the needed page once per missing operation (coalesced) and keeps the doc IDs and device id in memory.
  - **Shape:** threads have `thread_fbid` (URL and send id), legacy `thread_id` (read mutation), `thread_title`, `users` (without the viewer; Instagram `id` plus messaging `interop_messaging_user_fbid`), `viewer`, `slide_read_receipts` (`participant_fbid`, `watermark_timestamp_ms`) and `slide_messages` (newest first). Inbox message nodes carry `id` but not `message_id`. Content is `SlideMessageText`, `SlideMessageImageContent` (`attachments[]` with `attachment_cdn_url`, `preview_cdn_url`, `preview_width/height`), `SlideMessageXMAContent` (`xma` with `header_title_text`, `title_text`, `eyebrow_text`, `target_url`, `preview_image`; `xma_text_body` holds story-reply text), `SlideMessageAdminText` (`text_fragments`), videos and others. Reactions are `reactions[] {reaction, sender_fbid}`. Inbox messages only have `text_body` and an `igd_snippet` such as "X sent a photo.".
  - **Probe:** `scripts/instagram-web-client.py` `direct-inbox [--pages N]`, `direct-thread <thread_fbid> [--cursor C]`, `direct-seen <thread_fbid> <message_id>` and `direct-send-text <thread_fbid> <text> [--reply-to mid]`, all validated live (a test "test" DM was sent with the account owner's approval; read was marked on already-read threads).
  - **Native:** `InstagramDirect.swift` holds the DTOs (decoded with `convertFromSnakeCase` after pulling out the root field) and the client calls. Feed DMs use the inbox (first page, 15 threads) with the same rules as before: the newest message is from someone else and newer than the viewer's read receipt (or the thread is marked unread); `MESSAGE_INLINE_SHARE` is the media-share filter. Item ids are now `instagram:direct:<thread_fbid>:<mid>`.
  - **Chats:** the Chats list shows the newest 35 Instagram threads (two inbox requests per reload) with unread counts over each thread's 5 newest messages. `InstagramChatViewModel` opens a thread in the shared chat screen: newest 20 messages, older pages on scroll, a 20-second poll while open (one request each), text sending and swipe-to-reply, reactions shown read-only, photos in the image viewer, shared posts/reels/stories as cards that open Instagram, admin messages as captions, and the read receipt moved (debounced 600 ms) when an unread incoming message is shown. Unlike the feed fetch, opening a chat marks it read, matching Spotify and Apron chats.
  - Verified with `InstagramDirectTests` (synthetic fixtures), `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, and on the simulator with the diagnose credentials (loaded into the UserDefaults credential fallback and `account.instagram`): Instagram threads appear on the Chats screen and a thread shows text, story replies and photos.
- Investigated Spotify Messages (chats used to share tracks) and added read-only probe commands. Messages is mobile-only: the web player bundle declares a `messaging` right-sidebar panel but force-closes it, and none of its lazy chunks contain a chat client. The endpoints were found by statically decompiling the Android app (`com.spotify.music`, jadx; the APK was never run). The feature codename is "campfire" and the backend service is **speechless**, exposed as a Retrofit interface of `POST` calls on `spclient.wg.spotify.com` with protobuf bodies.
  - **Auth:** the existing WebPlayer bearer token (the one buddylist uses, no `client-token`) is accepted. The request must use `Content-Type: application/x-protobuf`; `application/protobuf` returns HTTP 400.
  - **Read endpoints (live-verified):** `speechless/v1/retrieve/chats-share-set/latest` takes `RetrieveLatestNodeRequest{1 sort_order, 2 limit}` and returns every chat with its latest message. `speechless/v1/retrieve/hierarchy` takes `RetrieveNodeRequest{1 hierarchy_id, 5 sort_order, 8 page_token, 9 page_size}` and returns one chat's messages newest-first, plus `next_page_token` (paging verified).
  - **Other endpoints (not called):** `retrieve/hierarchy/shares`, `submit/request` (`ClientNodeRequest`: sending messages, reactions and receipts), group create/rename/add/remove/leave, mute/unmute, read-receipt settings, invites, `can-message-users`, and `hierarchy/delete`.
  - **Response shape:** a chat is a `Hierarchy`. Its id is the base62 part of `spotify:chat:<id>`; `hierarchy_metadata.hierarchy_type` is DM or group; member usernames are in both `group.group_member[]` and an unnamed field 2; `hierarchy_statistic.total_user_unseen_events` counts unseen events. A message is a `ClientNode` with `node_identifier.uri` (`spotify:chat-message:<id>`), `event_capture.init_username` (sender), `event_capture.event_capture.occurred_at`, and `message_payload`.
  - **Message types:** contributions are `tracked_share{uri}` for in-app shares, `op_tracked_share{uri, op_share_id, destination}` for shares sent through a link such as `copy-link`, `free_text{message_text}`, and others (rich media, chat events, invites, taste-match cards). Reactions and receipts such as "played" arrive as `child_node`s of the message they refer to. Shared URIs can carry a `?si=` query suffix.
  - **Schema caveats:** the probe's schema was rebuilt from protobuf-lite `*_FIELD_NUMBER` constants, so names and numbers are reliable but some nested types are not. `NodeIdentifier.node_class` arrives as a length-delimited wrapper, not a varint enum. The decoder therefore decodes a field generically (as `#<n>`) whenever its wire type contradicts the schema.
  - **Probe:** `scripts/spotify-web-client.py` gained `chats [--limit N]` and `chat <id|spotify:chat:URI> [--page-size N] [--page-token T]`. Both are read-only and output normalized summaries plus the full decoded tree. The decoding uses a dependency-free protobuf encoder/decoder, a `SPEECHLESS_SCHEMA` subset, and `speechless_post`, which retries after a token refresh on 401/403.
  - **Not done yet:** real-time updates (the app gets them through Spotify's push channel, so the feed polls `latest` on each refresh instead) and any mutation through `submit/request`. Per `AGENTS.md`, a mutation needs its own probe validation first.
- Added Spotify Messages to the notifications feed. New `SpotifyMessagesSource` (`NotificationFetching`, registered in `AppContainer`) makes one `chats-share-set/latest` request per refresh (20 chats) and shows each chat's latest message as a `.message` item when someone else sent it within the last 7 days and it is a share or a text. The item stays whether or not it was seen in Spotify (product choice: same rule as Apron rooms, not Instagram's unread-only DMs). Rich media, chat events, invites and taste-match cards are skipped.
  - **Parsing:** `SpotifyMessagesParser` holds a minimal protobuf reader/writer and decodes only the fields the feed uses: chat id, DM/group type, group name and members, and the latest message's id, sender, `occurred_at`, and `tracked_share`/`op_tracked_share`/`free_text` content.
  - **Client:** `SpotifyClient.makeRequest` sends a POST with `application/x-protobuf` when given a protobuf body, and keeps the existing 401/403 token-refresh retry.
  - **Enrichment:** share titles and artwork come from the public oEmbed endpoint `GET https://open.spotify.com/oembed?url=<open.spotify.com URL>` (no auth; works for tracks, episodes and playlists; it has no artist). The probe's new `share-metadata <uri>` command covers it. Senders resolve through the existing `user-profile-view/v3/profile/<username>`. Both are best-effort and cached in memory per URL and username, so failures fall back to the raw username and "shared a track"-style text.
  - **Item shape:** a share's text is `<name> shared “<title>”`, its target URL is the `open.spotify.com/<type>/<id>` URL with any `?si=` suffix removed, and the artwork is `target.imageURL`, so the feed row shows it as the thumbnail. A text message is `<name> sent a message` with the message as the target text. Spotify usernames are often random IDs, so the actor keeps the username as `id`, leaves `username` nil (the feed prefers `username` when present), and uses the profile name as `displayName`. Profile navigation still works through the id. Item ids are `spotify:chat:<chatId>:<messageId>`, and disconnecting Spotify already clears `.spotify` cache items.
  - **Viewer:** the source needs the viewer's username (`metadataStore.spotifyAccount.username`) to tell incoming messages from your own. When it is missing it throws a service error rather than guessing.
  - **Track previews:** the detail screen of a shared Spotify track has a "Play Preview" button in its own section. It fetches the clip with the existing `SpotifyClient.trackPreviewURL` (embed page `audioPreview`, also covered by the probe's `track-preview`) and plays it with `AVPlayer` under the `.playback` audio session, the same as the story viewer. The button toggles to "Pause Preview", resets at the end of the clip, shows "No Preview Available" when the embed page has none, and stops playback when the screen disappears. `NotificationDetailView` now takes the `SpotifyClient` from `FeedView`.
  - Verified with `SpotifyMessagesParserTests` (synthetic protobuf fixtures, no real data), the full suite via `xcodebuild test` (90 tests, 1 skipped, 0 failures), `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, and on the simulator. The latest incoming share appears in the feed with the sender's avatar and name, the Spotify badge, and album artwork. Its detail screen shows the artwork, and the sender opens their Spotify profile.

## 2026-09-13

- Enabled the App Review preview content in **release** builds. Removed the `#if DEBUG` gates from the demo/preview path (`DemoData`, `FeedService` demo-mode checks, `StoryBarViewModel.loadDemoStoryBarItems`/`clearDemoStoryItems` and the demo-mode guards that skip real mutations, `AppContainer`, `SettingsView`) so App Store reviewers can reveal the Settings About heading (four taps) and choose **Load preview content**. Loading/unloading preview content leaves the real notification cache and credentials untouched, and while preview mode is active story/notification mutations (likes, seen state) are skipped. The Settings Demo Data section was simplified (no icons, added an explanatory footer) and the dev toggle no longer auto-loads preview content on enable — it only loads on the explicit button. The feed empty-state **Open Settings** moved from a `NavigationLink` to an `onOpenSettings` closure. Recorded the requirement in `docs/PLAN.md` under **App Review Preview Access**.

- Fixed GitHub silently showing no feed items once the stored session aged out (root-caused from a report of no GitHub content after being connected ~2 weeks). **It was not a parser/regex failure.** GitHub's `user_session` cookie has a two-week rolling lifetime; when it expires or is revoked, `GET /conduit/for_you_feed` answers **HTTP 302 → `https://github.com/login?return_to=…`**. `URLSession` follows that redirect and returns **HTTP 200 with the login HTML page** at `github.com/login`, so `GitHubClient.forYouFeed` (which only rejected 401/403) accepted it as a valid feed; `GitHubActivityParser.parse` then found zero `data-hydro-view` cards, yielding zero stories/notifications. Because no error was thrown, the account stayed `.valid`, so the UI showed nothing instead of prompting a reconnect.
  - Evidence from the booted simulator's WebKit cookie jar: `user_session`/`__Host-user_session_same_site` were captured 2026-08-28 and expired 2026-09-11 (exactly two weeks), while `saved_user_sessions` (`<user_id>:<session_key>`) expires 2026-11-26 (~3 months). A live probe while the session was still server-valid returned 200 with the feed; after it was revoked, the feed returned 302 to `/login` and the homepage returned `logged_in=no`. `saved_user_sessions` alone does **not** re-authenticate (sending it without `user_session` renders the login form), so there is no server-side refresh from the stored cookie values — the only way to get a fresh `user_session` is to log in again through the WebView.
  - **Python first:** `scripts/github-web-client.py` gained a `validate` subcommand backed by a new `NoRedirectHandler`, which requests the feed without following redirects and reports a structured diagnostic (`status` = `valid` / `expired` / `error`, plus `http_status`, `location`, and a `feed_item_count` when valid). The existing `feed` command already detected the login redirect via its final-URL check. `python3 scripts/github-web-client.py --simulator validate` now reports `"status": "expired"` with the `/login` location.
  - **Swift port:** added `GitHubClient.isLoginRedirect(_:)` (a GitHub response whose final URL path is `/login`) and made both `forYouFeed` and `authenticatedUser` throw `SourceError.notConfigured` on it, matching the existing 401/403 handling. `GitHubActivitySource.fetchGitHubActivity` and `GitHubNotificationSource.fetchNotifications` now catch `notConfigured`, call `markInvalid()`, and rethrow — mirroring `InstagramNotificationSource.fetchNotifications`. This flips the stored account to `.invalidCredentials`, which `GitHubConnectionView` already renders as a "Log in to GitHub" reconnect button.
  - Covered by two new `GitHubClientTests` cases (`testTreatsLoginRedirectAsNotConfigured`, `testAuthenticatedUserTreatsLoginRedirectAsNotConfigured`) using a new `GitHubURLProtocolStub.responseURL` hook to simulate the post-redirect final URL. Verified with `python3 -m py_compile` + live `validate` against the stale simulator session, `swiftformat` (0 files changed), `stupid-app build`, and `xcodebuild test` on the iOS 26.3 simulator (9/9 `GitHubClientTests`; full suite 60/61 with the only failure the pre-existing flaky 0.55s concurrency timing assertion in `FeedServiceTests`, which passes on rerun). End-to-end on the simulator: loading the stale credentials and pull-to-refreshing now flips `account.github.status` from `valid` to `invalidCredentials`.
  - Added an `AGENTS.md` Network Integration rule requiring network behavior to be diagnosed and validated in the dependency-free Python client first, before implementing the change in Swift.

- Investigated whether the GitHub web session can be **auto-refreshed without a WebView**. Findings:
  - GitHub's own session model (GitHub blog, "Modeling your App's User Session") marks a server-side session active while `accessed_at >= 2.weeks.ago`, and `accessed_at` is bumped on every authenticated request. The `user_session` cookie's two-week Max-Age is therefore client-side only: because the native client replays the cookie value instead of relying on a browser cookie jar, the same value keeps working past its stated expiry while the server-side session is kept active. (Observed directly: the sim's `user_session` expired 2026-09-11 but the feed still returned 200 on 2026-09-13.)
  - There is no refresh token and no server-side re-mint. With a live browser session captured, the feed with the full cookie set minus `user_session` (keeping `_gh_sess`, `_device_id`, `dotcom_user`, `_octo`, `logged_in`) returned **401**; adding a synthesized `saved_user_sessions=<uid>:<session_key>` plus a fresh `_gh_sess` also returned 401. Reloading `github.com` in a real browser re-issued only `_gh_sess`; GitHub never sent `Set-Cookie: user_session=`.
  - Conclusion: "auto refresh" means **keep the session active**, not mint a new one — an authenticated request at least every ~2 weeks. Foreground refresh already does this; a session that has fully lapsed or been revoked can only be recovered by logging in again (the detection/reconnect fix from the note above covers that case).
  - Implemented **foreground keep-alive support**: the login WebView now captures the full GitHub cookie set (`GitHubCredentials.additionalCookies`, an optional field so existing saved credentials still decode) and `GitHubClient` replays everything except the per-request `_gh_sess`. `GitHubClient.refreshedSessionCookies(from:)` parses any `user_session`/`__Host-user_session_same_site` GitHub re-issues via `Set-Cookie` into `GitHubForYouFeedResponse.refreshedUserSession`/`refreshedSameSiteUserSession`, and both GitHub sources persist a refreshed pair. No background task was added (foreground-only was chosen), so a user who does not open the app for 2+ weeks will still see the reconnect prompt.
  - Python first: `scripts/github-web-client.py` now replays `additionalCookies` and reports `refreshed_user_session`; validated live by replaying the browser's full cookie set (200, 27 items, no refresh). Covered by `testReplaysAdditionalCapturedCookiesButNotStaleSessionStore`, `testCapturesRefreshedSessionCookieFromSetCookie`, `testReplacingSessionCookiesPreservesAdditionalCookies`, and updated `CookieHeaderParserTests`. Verified with `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, and the full iOS test suite (64 tests, 1 skipped, 0 failures).

## 2026-09-04

- Released **1.0.0 (124)** to external TestFlight (build upload `432ce44b-6f29-42ce-bd7e-0dbef8da20a4`; IPA SHA-256 `099cb2fd…14e1`) into the **External** group (`ac67336d-4435-4ad7-9c6d-17ef19701dd1`). Set per-build "What's New"/What-to-Test note covering the changelog since build 121: X reply/mention/quote username fix, provider-side read marking, one-person-starring-many-repos fix, single-home navigation, and the pull-to-refresh-unconfigured fix described below. The `external-beta` CLI poll timed out after creating the submission, but live ASC state confirmed external `IN_BETA_TESTING`.

## 2026-09-04

- Fixed pull-to-refresh failing with a "Refresh Issue" alert when fewer social accounts than the full set were connected. Root cause: `FeedService.manualRefresh()` appended `"<network> is not configured"` for every unconfigured notification source to its `errors` array, and then threw `SourceError.serviceError(...)` whenever `!errors.isEmpty && incoming.isEmpty`. With only one network connected (e.g. Spotify), the unconfigured sources made `errors` non-empty, and if the connected source returned no items that pull-to-refresh threw. Now unconfigured (`SourceError.notConfigured`) and endpoint-spike-pending sources are logged/skipped but never counted as refresh failures, so `errors` only contains genuine runtime failures. The throw-iff-empty-and-failed fallback survives for real failures. Verified with `swiftformat` and `stupid-app build`.

## 2026-09-03

- Released internal TestFlight build **1.0.0 (123)** containing provider-side notification read marking and the single-home-screen navigation redesign. Archived IPA SHA-256 `9c39d44f…fbc8`; App Store Connect build upload `a0c7c2d6-73e8-4c2c-b31e-d846eb40d53d` completed as `VALID` and `IN_BETA_TESTING`. Before upload, the packaged release binary was checked for both new provider endpoint markers (`notifications/all/last_seen_cursor.json` and `app.bsky.notification.updateSeen`).

- **Reverted** an attempt to vertically center the Search screen's empty state: wrapping the `List` in a `GeometryReader` and sizing the empty row to `geometry.size.height` made the transition to/from the empty state animate jankily on iOS 26 (and the first attempt using `.containerRelativeFrame(.vertical)` crashed with a Swift assertion inside `UICollectionView` layout invalidation). The empty state is back to its original fixed-height row (`.frame(minHeight: 320)`); centering is left as-is.

- Replaced the tab navigation (bottom `TabView` on compact widths / `NavigationSplitView` sidebar on regular widths) with a single home screen. `ContentView` now owns the root `NavigationStack` (path-driven via `[HomeDestination]`) and renders `FeedView` as its root; Search and Settings are pushed destinations registered via `.navigationDestination(for:)` on the home root, and SearchView/SettingsView dropped their internal `NavigationStack` wrappers and sheet-only Done buttons.
  - Search entry point: Search is a top-right toolbar button (magnifyingglass, `NavigationLink(value: .search)`) to the left of the Settings gear. It pushes `SearchView`, which itself uses the **native system search bar** (`searchable(text: $viewModel.query, placement: .automatic, prompt: "Search")`) — on iOS 26 this renders as the Liquid Glass search field at the bottom of the Search screen, exactly the standard system field. The network filter menu stays in the Search screen's top-right toolbar (`.primaryAction`). (A filter-to-bottom experiment was attempted and reverted: putting the filter in `.bottomBar` with `DefaultToolbarItem(kind: .search, placement: .bottomBar)` draws the leading item *inside* the search capsule, and floating it above the bar via a bottom `safeAreaInset` still looked wrong — the user reverted it, so the filter remains top-right.) This replaced the previous custom top `TextField` strip (`.onChange(of: viewModel.query)` debounced search is preserved). Two intermediate search-entry designs were also tried and abandoned: a bottom-of-home custom search affordance (NavigationLink) and a home-screen `searchable` field whose activation was intercepted (`isPresented`/`searchFocused` onChange → cancel presentation + push `.search`); the final call moved the native field onto the Search screen itself and back to a plain toolbar button on home. Verified on the iOS 26.3 simulator: home top-right shows search + settings, both push; Search screen shows the native bottom search field with the filter menu in its top-right toolbar; back returns home. Note: `simctl install` of `.build/arm64-apple-ios-simulator/debug/` silently installs a STALE simulator binary — only `stupid-app run --simulator` assembles/installs the fresh one (documented in the 08-31 demo-data note too).
  - Settings entry point: gear toolbar button stays in the feed's trailing toolbar (with the red attention dot from the former Settings tab badge), and the empty-state `Open Settings` action is a `NavigationLink(value: .settings)`.

- Changed successful notification loads to clear provider-side unread state for X and Bluesky, while preserving Instagram DM unread state. Browser network inspection showed that X's `NotificationsTimeline` GraphQL fetch does not issue a read write on its own. The current web bundle exposes `POST /i/api/2/notifications/{type}/last_seen_cursor`; live probing established that the `cursor` must be the opaque `TimelineTimelineCursor` value whose `cursorType` is `Top` (the numeric notification `sortIndex` is rejected with code 358). `scripts/x-web-client.py load-notifications` now fetches, advances `POST /i/api/2/notifications/all/last_seen_cursor.json?cursor=<top-cursor>`, and verifies unread count; the live request succeeded and returned unread count 0. `XClient.notifications()` now performs the same write after successful decoding/parsing.
- Bluesky uses the official AT Protocol `app.bsky.notification.updateSeen` procedure against the user's PDS with a DPoP-authenticated JSON body `{"seenAt":"<ISO-8601>"}`. Browser capture of bsky.app confirmed that exact call after `listNotifications`; `scripts/bluesky-web-client.py load-notifications` was implemented first and verified live, with a follow-up list returning `isRead: true` for all fetched notifications. `BlueskyClient.notifications()` now marks seen after a successful list response. Instagram's web notifications route issues the same `POST /api/v1/news/inbox/` request already used by the native client and no separate activity-seen mutation; the discovered DM read endpoint is intentionally not used, per product decision. GitHub is excluded and Farcaster remains unsupported because Hypersnap seen writes return 501. Verified with Python compilation/linting, `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, a simulator install/launch and pull-to-refresh, provider-side checks (X unread count 0 and Bluesky fetched items all `isRead: true`), and all 59 iOS tests (1 skipped, 0 failures).

## 2026-09-02

- Made X reply/mention/quote notification text use the **username** consistently instead of the tweet author's display name. `XNotificationsTimelineParser.parseTweetEntry` previously built the summary from `author.name` (e.g. "dutch replied", "Andrey 🦃 Petrov replied"); it now prefers `actor.username` (e.g. "ionodutch replied", "alexanderchopan quoted"), matching like/follow rows which already read @username. Verified in the simulator cache.

- Fixed missing GitHub "starred your repository" notifications when **one person stars many of your repos**. GitHub's For You feed renders this as a rollup: a head card (`card_sub_position` 0) that names the actor — "Sandalots starred 5 of your repositories" — while only rendering the first repo's body, plus actor-less sibling cards (sub_positions 1..N) that each carry one extra repo (in a broken "Uh oh!" body). Both the record/hydro parser and the previous text aggregator paired an actor↔repo only when both appeared in the same `<article>`, so it recovered just the head's repo (e.g. 2 of 5). `GitHubActivitySource.aggregatedYourRepositoryItems` now reads each card's `card_position`/`card_sub_position` (from the encoded hydro JSON) and propagates the head (sub_position 0) actor to its actor-less siblings at the same `card_position`, so all N repos surface. Verified against the live feed for both directions: `Sandalots → rpc-racer, search-bangs-worker, stupid-app-cli, agent-cal, ipfs-racer` (5) and `aaronjmars → +stupid-upload` (6), matching the browser. Ported the same logic into `scripts/github-web-client.py::extract_starred_your_repo` first (probe validated live) and confirmed parity with a standalone Swift trace of the real feed HTML. Added `testRollupExpandsSameActorAcrossManyRepos`. Verified with `swiftformat` and `stupid-app build`.

- GitHub story repo cards now show the star count to 3 significant figures. The star count was previously passed through from GitHub's `aria-label="… stargazers"` unchanged (an exact integer like `"63"`/`"1234"`), so large repos rendered as a long raw integer. `GitHubActivitySource.repoMetadata` now runs the captured raw count through a new `formattedStarCount` that parses it (handles thousands separators and any `k`/`m`/`b` suffix) and reformats to at most 3 significant figures with a `k`/`m`/`b` suffix — `63` → `63`, `1,234` → `1.23k`, `12,300` → `12.3k`, `104,000` → `104k`, `1,234,000` → `1.23m`. Trailing fractional zeros are stripped (`trimmingZeros`). Covered by a new `GitHubActivityParserTests.testFormatsRepoStarCountToThreeSignificantFigures`. While running the suite I also fixed a pre-existing compile break that blocked tests entirely: `StorySeenSyncTests` called `StorySeenSync.merged(local:remote:)` but the implementation signature is `merged(_:_:)` with no labels, so I dropped the labels to match. Verified with `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, and the full 58-test suite on the iOS simulator (1 skipped, 0 failures) via `xcodebuild test`.

- Shipped the star-count formatting to internal TestFlight as build `1.0.0 (121)` (bumped `CFBundleVersion` 120 → 121, `release archive` + `release upload --wait`); App Store Connect state `processing=VALID, internal=IN_BETA_TESTING`.

## 2026-09-02

- Fixed the actor ordering (and therefore the title) of grouped X engagement notifications (e.g. "… and N others liked your tweet"). Root cause: the feed fetched X from the **legacy `notifications/all.json`** endpoint, whose `fromUsers` array never reorders when a new like arrives, so it can't match the real display order. **Switched the X feed to the GraphQL `NotificationsTimeline` query** — the endpoint X's own web UI uses (`GET /i/api/graphql/8kmedJD_u653xqEjZIS7yA/NotificationsTimeline?…` with `notificationsTimelineFeatures()`). `XClient.notifications()` now decodes `XNotificationsTimelineResponse` and `XNotificationsTimelineParser` normalizes aggregate actions from `template.from_users` **in server order (no reversal)**, which is newest-like-first per X, and mention/reply/quote entries from `TimelineTweet` item content. Verified live in the simulator cache: the tweet `2095056315663929849` now reads `h3nryXYZ and 9 others liked your tweet` (H³nry — the newest like — first). The count-only `all/unread_count.json` poll and the `.post`-detail `notifications/device_follow.json` path are kept. X follow notifications were dropping because `XNotificationCategory.category(for: .follow)` returned `nil`, so this change added a `follows` case to `XNotificationCategory` and mapped `NotificationType.follow` to it, plus a one-time `account.x.followsMigrated` migration in `AccountMetadataStore` to enable the new category for accounts configured before it existed (a flag prevents overriding a later opt-out). Verified `blankspac_e followed you` now renders in the simulator feed.

- Rebuilt the distribution provisioning profile to authorize iCloud. Enabled the `ICLOUD` capability (setting `ICLOUD_VERSION`/`XCODE_6`) on bundle ID `tech.stupid.StupidSocial`, deleted the stale App Store profile (`BVYGFU98B5`), and regenerated it (`8H4YURJVKH`, UUID `dd1f3c34…`). The new profile authorizes `com.apple.developer.ubiquity-kvstore-identifier` (`<TeamID>.*`), `com.apple.developer.icloud-services` (`*`). This `signing setup` flow requires the build to ship with `stupid-app` 0.1.x containing the `EntitlementDeriver` fix (commit `7607cf6` in the `stupid-ios-dev` repo): the deriver now substitutes `$(TeamIdentifierPrefix)`/`$(CFBundleIdentifier)` and authorizes concrete String entitlement values against profile-side trailing-`.*` grants (matching `codesign`). Without that CLI fix, `release archive` fails with "ubiquity-kvstore-identifier value mismatch." Verified `release archive` signs and embeds `ubiquity-kvstore-identifier = <TEAM>.tech.stupid.StupidSocial`.

## 2026-09-02

- Added cross-device sync of Spotify and GitHub story *seen* state through iCloud Key-Value storage. New `StorySeenSync` (in `NoFeedSocialCore/Storage/StorySeenSync.swift`) mirrors the two local seen dictionaries (`spotifyActivitySeenTimestamps`, keyed by `spotify:user:` URI, and `githubActivitySeenTimestamps`, keyed by `actorId`) into `NSUbiquitousKeyValueStore` under `com.stupid.storyseen.spotify` / `com.stupid.storyseen.github`. Seen timestamps are monotonic per actor, so each end does an element-wise `max` merge (`StorySeenSync.merged`) and writes the converged result back to both local `UserDefaults` and iCloudKey, giving convergence despite iCloud's last-writer-wins KV semantics. `StoryBarViewModel` pushes to iCloud after `markSpotifyActivityAsSeen`/`markGitHubActivityAsSeen`, observes `StorySeenSync.didChangeNotification`, and re-derives flags via a new `refreshSeenState()` that extracts shared `spotifySortedUnreadFirst`/`githubSortedUnreadFirst` orderers. The local `UserDefaults` stores remain the fast read path, so the feature degrades to local-only when iCloud is unavailable. Added the `com.apple.developer.ubiquity-kvstore-identifier` entitlement (`$(TeamIdentifierPrefix)$(CFBundleIdentifier)`) to `App.entitlements` — this is a new capability beyond the existing Keychain-only setup, so the provisioning profile must include iCloud/Key-Value storage for archive/release builds. Added `StorySeenSyncTests` for the `max`-merge. Verified with `swiftformat` and `stupid-app build`.

## 2026-09-02

- Added GitHub story slide image preloading to `UnifiedStoryViewer`. `prepareForCurrentItem()` now also calls `preloadAdjacentGitHubStories()` (mirroring the existing Instagram/Spotify adjacent-preload pattern), warming the avatar images the next GitHub slide will render before it appears: the current group's previous/next slide and the first/last slides of the adjacent groups. For a `starred/created/followed/unknown` slide that's `activity.targetAvatarURL`; for a `forkedRepository` slide it preloads `activity.targetAvatarURL` **and** the original repo's owner avatar (`https://github.com/<owner>.png?size=192`, resolved from `GitHubOriginalCache`) since the fork card swaps to the owner avatar once its OpenGraph data resolves. Forks that resolve during the slide (via `GitHubOriginalCache.load`/`preload`) also kick off that owner-avatar download through a new `store(_:forFork:)` step, so the card's image is cached by the time the user advances. Verified with `swiftformat` and `stupid-app build`.

## 2026-09-01

- Released build **118** (marketing 1.0.0) to external TestFlight carrying the story-viewer keyboard navigation (Left/Right prev/next slide, Shift+Left/Right prev/next user, Space pause) and the swipe-to-Open-Post row action from this build. Bumped `CFBundleVersion` 117→118, archived (`.release/NoFeedSocial.ipa`, SHA-256 `e7bb1fec…51b9e`), upload build `33bfe86d-010b-4675-bfcc-6c5c797b3e6d` (VALID, internal IN_BETA_TESTING). `stupid-app release external-beta` added it to the "External Testers" group `c0455359-6a5e-4691-a5e9-47e91e4351b0`, set the "What to Test" note, and created the external beta review submission — the CLI's poll timed out before Apple's beta review returned, but live ASC confirmed the submission is APPROVED (`BETA_APPROVED`) and the build↔external-group link is present, so the build is externally testable.
- Added keyboard navigation to the unified story viewer (`UnifiedStoryViewer`). Left/Right changes the prev/next slide (reusing the existing `goBack()`/`advance()`), Shift+Left/Shift+Right jumps to the previous/next user story (`goToPreviousItem()`/`goToNextItem()`), and Space toggles the slide timer pause (`togglePause()`, which also pauses/resumes the Instagram player). Wired as hidden `.keyboardShortcut` buttons in a zero-sized background, mirroring the existing Cmd+R pattern: `.keyboardShortcut` routes through hardware `UIKeyCommand`s, so it works with external iPad keyboards and the Mac-compatibility path, and is inert for touch-only onscreen typing.

- Added a right-to-left swipe-to-"Open Post" action on notification feed rows that opens the target post URL. Extracted the post-URL resolution that previously lived only in `NotificationDetailView.postURL(for:)` into a shared `NotificationTargetURL.resolve(network:target:)` helper in `AppModels.swift` so the detail view and the new swipe action share identical logic. The resolver keeps the same per-network behavior: Farcaster builds a `farcaster.xyz/~/conversations/<hash>` (later switched to `quilcast.com/c/<hash>`) URL from the target id, X builds `x.com/i/status/<id>`, and Instagram/GitHub/Bluesky/Spotify use the target's direct `url`. Follows and targets without a `url` expose no swipe action. `FeedView` now holds an `@Environment(\.openURL)` and attaches a trailing swipe action, `allowsFullSwipe` for a quick shortcut.

- GitHub story reels now use the same "resume at the oldest un-viewed slide" logic as Instagram when opening the story viewer. `StoryBarViewModel.storyViewerStartSlideIndex` previously returned `0` for every non-Instagram item; it now returns the first activity whose timestamp is after the actor's stored seen timestamp (from `GitHubActivitySeenStore`), falling back to `0` when the group is fully seen. `GitHubActivitySeenStore` exposes the raw stored timestamp via a new `seenTimestamp(actorId:)` accessor. Refactored the shared resume-offset computation into a private `resumeIndex(in:after:)` helper used by both Instagram and GitHub; Spotify stays single-slide (index 0). Added unit tests covering unread and fully-read GitHub groups.

## 2026-08-31

- Demo-data detail targets now carry an `author` (with a placeholder avatar) and a `media` image URL (`picsum.photos` 1200×1000), so opening a demo notification's detail renders an avatar and a media preview instead of an empty "Post"/"Message" section. Added the `media(_ seed:)` helper; the X reply, X like, Bluesky reply/like, Farcaster reply, and Instagram DM demo targets now resolve author images + media. The detail screenshot on the `APP_IPHONE_61` set grew from a sparse ~232 KB to ~1.2 MB, avoiding the "images missing" look for store/preview purposes.

- Added a debug-only **demo data mode** so the app can be populated with realistic-but-fake content for App Store screenshots and reviewer browsing without any live network credentials. All of it is gated behind `#if DEBUG`, so it is never compiled into the App Store build. `DemoData` (in `NoFeedSocialCore/Sources/DemoData.swift`) builds ~9 feed notifications (X reply/like/follow, Farcaster follow/reply, Bluesky reply/like, Instagram DM, GitHub star) plus story-bar content (2 Instagram reels, a Spotify track, and 2 GitHub groups) using deterministic placeholder avatars (`i.pravatar.cc`) / artwork (`picsum.photos`) that fall back to initials when offline.
  - `FeedService.loadDemoFeed()` seeds the SwiftData cache; `FeedService.manualRefresh()`/`foregroundActivationRefresh()` and `StoryBarViewModel.performStoryBarContentFetch()` short-circuit to demo content while `DemoData.isDemoMode` is set, so a normal refresh cannot wipe the preview with live data.
  - Activation: (1) launch arg `-demoData` or env `DEMO_DATA=1` auto-loads at startup; (2) toggling the hidden Debug toggle on (the 4-tap "About" header in Settings) **auto-loads** the demo content via `.onChange(of: devModeEnabled)` → `AppContainer.loadDemoData()`; toggling it off calls `AppContainer.clearDemoData()` which flips the demo flag off, empties the demo-seeded cache/story bar, and runs a live foreground refresh so real feed + stories return.
  - The Settings "Demo Data" section (shown when Debug is on) has three controls: **Load preview content**, **Unload preview content** (`clearDemoData()`), and a **Redact names** toggle backed by a new `@AppStorage("redactionEnabled")`. Name redaction (`DebugRedaction`) is now driven by `redactionEnabled` (default off), decoupled from `devModeEnabled` (which stays the debug-UI gate), so enabling demo mode no longer forces every name to "Redacted". Call sites in FeedView / NotificationDetailView / UnifiedStoryViewer / ProfileDetailView / SearchView were switched to the new key.
  - Note for future agent: `stupid-app build` emits the simulator app to `.build/arm64-apple-ios/debug/`, but the stale `.build/arm64-apple-ios-simulator/debug/` copy (older mtime) is what a naive `find ... ` installs. Use `stupid-app run --simulator` to build+install the correct simulator binary, then relaunch it with the env/arg flag to activate demo mode.

## 2026-08-30

- Fixed the Cmd+R refresh loading indicator not showing on the Mac-compatibility refresh path. `FeedView`'s toolbar `ProgressView` only showed while `isForegroundRefreshing` was true, but the global Cmd+R shortcut (`ContentView.refreshFeedAndStories`) calls `FeedViewModel.refresh()`, which flips `isRefreshing` — on iOS the native `.refreshable` spinner only appears during the pull gesture, so a keyboard-triggered refresh had no visible indicator. The toolbar indicator now shows for either `isRefreshing || isForegroundRefreshing`. Verified via `stupid-app build` and `run --simulator`.

- Fixed GitHub For You "created repository" events being dropped from the stories bar. The parser's `GitHubActivityKind` only mapped `STARRED_REPOSITORY`, `FOLLOW`, and `FORKED_REPOSITORY`, so GitHub's `CREATED_REPOSITORY` cards fell to `.unknown` and `normalize(_:)` returned nil. Added a `createdRepository = "CREATED_REPOSITORY"` kind and normalized it exactly like the other repo cards (actor is the creating USER, target is the new REPO): it now inherits the repo's description/language/stars, gets a "created" verb and a  "folder.badge.plus" story icon, and renders as a repo card in `GitHubStoryContent` (the existing non-fork/non-follow branch already handled it). It is not routed to the notifications feed (only starred-your-repo events are). Confirmed against the live feed, which currently carries 4 `CREATED_REPOSITORY` cards (`Lakr233`, `vrypan`, `deodad`, `kevoconnell`). Also taught `scripts/github-web-client.py` the `created` verb for `CREATED_REPOSITORY` so the probe's structured summaries match the app. Covered by a new `GitHubActivityParserTests` case and verified via `stupid-app build`.

- Fixed Spotify "Service error: Validation failed" showing on the TestFlight build (and cross-device/discovered sessions) while buddy activity still loads. Root cause: `SpotifyClient.validateAccount()` bundled two requests with different auth requirements — the spclient `presence-view/v1/buddylist` (used by the activity feed, works with only the cookie-refreshed bearer token) **and** a Pathfinder `api-partner.spotify.com/pathfinder/v2/query` (`profileAttributes`) username lookup that requires a `client-token` header. When the stored/expected `client-token` (or an expired/anonymous `/api/token`) caused Pathfinder to return HTTP 401/403, the whole account was marked `.serviceError` → "Validation failed", even though buddy activity (and therefore the account) was healthy.
  - Live probe on the reported session (`spclient --credentials`) confirmed: `buddylist` 200 ✔, but every Pathfinder call (`profile-attributes`, search, library) returned HTTP 401 "Missing/invalid/expired access token"; `/api/token` (reason=init/transport) succeeded but returned `isAnonymous: true` and carries **no username**; the public `api.spotify.com/v1/me` returned HTTP 429 (rate-limited). So an expired/anonymous session simply has no resolvable username — the buddy list works but the profile identity is gone.
  - Fix: account validity is now decided **only** by the buddylist request (the same spclient call the feed uses). Username resolution is best-effort: Pathfinder `userAttributes`, falling back to the stored `credentials.username`. A failed username lookup can no longer invalidate a working account. To make healthy-session resolution robust when a stored `client-token` has gone stale, `resolveUsername()` now retries once after minting a **fresh client-token** from `clienttoken.spotify.com/v1/clienttoken` using the `clientId` that `/api/token` (reason=init) returns — the probe confirmed no direct spotify public/me/**api** returns the username. Added an optional `clientId` field to `SpotifyCredentials` (decode-safe, defaulted nil) so the app can mint tokens.
  - Probe grew two diagnostic commands: `client-token` (mint a fresh Pathfinder client-token from the same session as buddylist) and `username` (mint a fresh token then resolve the username via Pathfinder), plus `current-username` (checks `GET api.spotify.com/v1/me` — noted it is rate-limited, not a reliable path).

## 2026-08-30

- Added a global `Command+R` refresh shortcut. `ContentView` attaches a hidden `Button` bound to `.keyboardShortcut("r", modifiers: .command)` in the root background; it calls the shared `refreshFeedAndStories(container:)` (feed + stories) from any pane. SwiftUI `.keyboardShortcut` routes through hardware `UIKeyCommand`s, so it works on iPad keyboards and the Mac-compatibility path (`stupid-app run --mac`); on iPhone it's inert unless a hardware keyboard is connected. Verified the app still builds/launches on both the iPhone (compact) and iPad (wide) simulators.

## 2026-08-30

- Added a wide, platform-adaptive layout driven by `horizontalSizeClass`, with no macOS-specific code: on `.regular` widths (now reachable on iPad and the Mac compatibility canvas) `ContentView` switches from the compact bottom `TabView` to a `NavigationSplitView` with a Home/Search/Settings sidebar (`wideLayout`) and the same three panes in the detail column (`detailView`); compact stays the existing bottom tab bar. Selection is centralised in `tabSelection` / `tabSelectionOptional` / `setSelectedTab`, keeping the refresh-on-re-selecting-Home behavior for both paths. To actually run at native iPad size (and reach `.regular`), `Info.plist`'s `UIDeviceFamily` was changed from `[1]` (iPhone-only, which made iPad run the app in 375×667 phone-boxed mode forced to `.compact`) to `[1, 2]` (iPhone + iPad). Verified on an iOS 26.3 iPad Pro 11 simulator: native ~680×1210 canvas, sidebar shows Home/Search/Settings, tapping Search loads the search pane; and on the iPhone simulator the bottom TabView is unchanged.

## 2026-08-30

- Removed the macOS target entirely — the app is now iOS-only. `Sources/NoFeedSocialMac/NoFeedSocialMacApp.swift` and its `NoFeedSocialMac` executable product/target were deleted from `Package.swift`, the `.macOS(.v26)` platform was dropped from the package manifest, and the `#if !os(macOS)` guard around `@main NoFeedSocialApp` in `Sources/NoFeedSocial/NoFeedSocialApp.swift` was removed so it is now the app's single unconditional entry point. `stupid-app build` still produces the iOS app successfully. `AGENTS.md` Project Structure, `README.md` (badges + "Run on macOS" section), `docs/PLAN.md`, and `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` were updated to the iOS-only scope. (This supersedes the earlier in-flight idea of fiddling with the macOS window's `defaultSize`; with macOS gone there is no window to size.) Removed every `#elseif os(macOS)` / `os(macOS)` platform branch and macOS-only `NSViewRepresentable`/`NSView`/`NSImage`/`NSApplication` fallback across the view layer (`CachedAsyncImage`, `NetworkBadgeIcon`, `FeedView`, `ProfileDetailView`, `NotificationDetailView`, `BlueskyConnectionView`, `StoryComposerView`, `UnifiedStoryViewer`, `XLoginWebView`, `GitHubLoginWebView`, `InstagramLoginWebView`, `SpotifyLoginWebView`, `FarcasterConnectionView`, `ContentView`), keeping the iOS `UIView…` primary branches and making `@main NoFeedSocialApp`'s `@available(iOS 16.4, *)` `isInspectable` check use iOS instead of mac. `targetEnvironment(simulator)` `#else` branches in `SettingsView` and `KeychainCredentialStore` were intentionally kept — they distinguish simulator vs device on iOS, not platform. Noted consequence: on a macOS host, bare `swift build`/`swift test` default to a macOS destination that the shared ViewModel layer no longer targets (`@Published` needs macOS 10.15+ now that `.macOS` is dropped from `Platforms`), so host-side SwiftPM builds are not a supported verification path anymore; iOS verification uses `stupid-app build`.

## 2026-08-29

- Tapping and holding now pauses the slide timer for GitHub stories too, matching Instagram stories. Holding already set `isPaused = true` in `UnifiedStoryViewer.storyGesture`, but only while `currentItem?.isInstagram`; GitHub has no audio player, so only the timer needed pausing (the `onReceive` tick returns early when `isPaused`). Added an `isGitHub` helper on `StoryBarItem` and paused on hold for `isInstagram || isGitHub`. Release already resets `isPaused = false` unconditionally (the `player?.play()` stays Instagram-only since GitHub plays no media).

## 2026-08-29

- Removed the per-connection "Credentials" storage-location row (it surfaced `iCloud` / `This device` via `*CredentialStorage` labels) from the X, Instagram, Spotify, Bluesky, and GitHub connection screens. The backing `*CredentialStorage` published properties remain because `hasLocalOnlyCredentials` still drives the Settings-wide local-credentials warning.
- Fixed GitHub so the signed-in account is resolved and persisted.
