If Instagram's story fetch fails transiently while Spotify succeeds, the unified story list previously replaced existing Instagram stories with a Spotify-only list. `InstagramNotificationSource.fetchStoryReels()` now throws on tray failures instead of collapsing them to an empty result, and `FeedViewModel.fetchStoryBarContent()` preserves existing Instagram stories for transient Instagram failures while still clearing them when Instagram is not configured.

### Story viewer swipe navigation

- Horizontal swipe gestures in `UnifiedStoryViewer` now jump directly between story users/items. Tap zones still navigate within the current Instagram user's slides, preserving familiar story behavior: tap advances slide, swipe advances user.
- Replaced the competing invisible button tap zones, pause gesture, and navigation gesture with one unified drag gesture. This allows horizontal swipes to be recognized anywhere in the viewer, preserves short tap left/right for slide navigation, and prevents a long-press pause from advancing/skipping when released.

### Instagram muted stories

- Live `reels_tray` payload inspection confirmed Instagram includes mute metadata in tray entries: top-level `muted`, plus `user.friendship_status.is_muting_reel` and `user.friendship_status.muting`. `InstagramTrayItem` now decodes these fields and `InstagramNotificationSource.fetchStoryReels()` filters muted tray entries before deduping users or fetching story media.
- Live `reels_tray` payload inspection confirmed Instagram exposes close-friends story availability as `has_besties_media` and `latest_besties_reel_media` on tray entries. `reels_media` did not expose a matching per-slide close-friends field in a 12-reel/61-item sample. `InstagramStoryReel` now carries `hasCloseFriendsMedia`, and Instagram story bubbles render a green star badge at the bottom-right when `has_besties_media` is true.
- Unread Instagram Close Friends stories now sort before other unread stories in both Instagram-only reel ordering and the unified stories bar. Seen ordering remains timestamp-based after the existing seen/unseen split.
- Debugged story posting failure from simulator CFNetwork logs: the image upload request with the large story body returned HTTP 403 before `configure_to_story` ran. `publishPhotoStory` now uses cookie-authenticated mobile headers for `/rupload_igphoto/...` and keeps the mobile `Authorization: Bearer IGT:2:...` header for the configure step. Publish failures now surface the failed step/status in the composer instead of a generic retry message.
- Fixed the follow-up `configure_to_story` HTTP 403 `login_required` failure: live auth probes showed Instagram accepts the mobile `Authorization: Bearer IGT:2:<base64-json>` header only when the base64 padding is preserved. The app no longer strips trailing `=` padding from that bearer payload.

## 2026-05-12

### Story composer scaffold

- Added a presentation-only story composer opened from the first bubble in the stories bar. The composer starts with Instagram as the displayed target, has a top-right vertical editing toolbar with image selection as the first tool, and fits a selected local image centered on the fullscreen canvas.
- Added a text tool as the second composer toolbar action. Each tap creates a new centered editable text caption; captions move live while dragged around the story canvas after editing, and tapping the canvas outside captions clears focus. On iOS, dragging is backed by small UIKit overlays so pan updates move native text views directly instead of triggering SwiftUI layout on every frame. State is kept local to the composer.
- No upload, submit, account-selection, or cross-posting network logic was added; the composer state is local UI scaffolding only.

### Instagram story posting investigation

- Inspected `third-party/instagram-429-0-0-32-70.apk` via DEX strings because `jadx`/`apktool` are not installed. The APK contains the same private posting endpoints used by `instagram-private-api`: `/rupload_igphoto/{name}`, `/rupload_igvideo/{name}`, `/api/v1/media/configure/`, `/api/v1/media/configure_sidecar/`, `/api/v1/media/configure_to_story/`, and `/api/v1/media/configure_to_clips/`.
- The checked-in `third-party/instagram-cli/node_modules/instagram-private-api/dist` has the clearest request reference. Story photo publishing is a two-step flow: upload JPEG bytes to `/rupload_igphoto/{upload_id}_0_{random}` with `X-Instagram-Rupload-Params` containing `media_type=1`, `upload_id`, `retry_context`, `xsharing_user_ids=[]`, and JPEG compression metadata; then `POST /api/v1/media/configure_to_story/` with a signed form containing the upload id, dimensions, `source_type=3`, `configure_mode=1`, `client_shared_at`, `edits.crop_original_size`, `edits.crop_center`, and `edits.crop_zoom`.
- Story video publishing is longer: upload MP4 to `/rupload_igvideo/{name}` using video rupload params (`media_type=2`, dimensions, duration, upload id, retry context), upload a cover image to `/rupload_igphoto/` with the same upload id, call the upload-finish endpoint with `source_type=3` and video length, then `POST /api/v1/media/configure_to_story/?video=1` with a signed form including `clips`, `extra.source_width`, `extra.source_height`, `audio_muted`, `poster_frame_index`, dimensions, length, and `configure_mode=1`.
- Close Friends story publishing uses `audience=besties` in the configure-to-story form. Direct story sends use `configure_mode=2` with `recipient_users`, `thread_ids`, `view_mode`, `reply_type`, and `client_context` instead of normal story audience publishing.
- Sticker/link metadata is serialized into configure-to-story fields such as `reel_mentions`, `story_hashtags`, `story_locations`, `story_polls`, `story_sliders`, `story_questions`, `story_countdowns`, `story_cta`, `attached_media`, `story_chats`, `story_quizs`, and `story_sticker_ids`. Link stickers are represented as `story_cta=[{ links: [{ webUri: ... }] }]`.
- Configure requests are `signed_body` form posts using the existing Instagram HMAC key/version (`ig_sig_key_version=4`), matching the signing path already implemented for `markStorySeen`. Upload requests are raw `application/octet-stream` bodies with explicit `Content-Length`, `X-Entity-*` headers, and `X-Instagram-Rupload-Params` JSON.
- No live post/upload test was performed during this investigation to avoid creating visible Instagram content or leaving server-side uploaded media without explicit approval.

### Instagram story deletion investigation

- Inspected the APK DEX strings and `instagram-private-api` deletion references. The shared media deletion endpoint is `POST /api/v1/media/{mediaId}/delete/?media_type={PHOTO|VIDEO|CAROUSEL}`. The signed form contains `igtv_feed_preview=false`, `media_id`, `_csrftoken`, `_uid`, and `_uuid`.
- The APK also contains story-adjacent deletion endpoints and actions: `media/%s/delete/`, `media/%s/delete/?media_type=%s`, `media/%s/deleted_info/`, `media/%s/delete_stitched_media_story_parts/`, `media/%s/delete_story_countdown/`, `media/story_comment/delete/`, `media/%s/async_delete_story_poll_reply/`, `media/%s/async_delete_story_quiz_reply/`, and `stories/prompt_stickers/delete_story_template/`. For deleting a normal active story item, the generic media delete endpoint is the primary candidate.
- `instagram-private-api` exposes `media.delete({ mediaId, mediaType })` for feed/photo/video/carousel media and `highlights.deleteReel(highlightId)` for deleting an entire highlight reel via `POST /api/v1/highlights/{highlightId}/delete_reel/` with a signed `_csrftoken`/`_uid`/`_uuid` form.
- Direct-message story/item deletion is separate from public story deletion: `POST /api/v1/direct_v2/threads/{threadId}/items/{itemId}/delete/` with `_csrftoken` and `_uuid` only.
- No live delete test was performed. Before implementing destructive deletion in the app, test against a throwaway story created explicitly for this purpose and verify whether Instagram expects `media_type=PHOTO` for image stories and `media_type=VIDEO` for video stories, or whether story media accepts either value based on the media id.

### Login region and verification hardening

- Investigated user feedback that Instagram requested many verification steps and Spotify could not find the account. No South Africa-specific region lock was present, but the login flows had hard-coded locale/browser identity hints: Spotify used `/en/login` with a fixed iPhone Safari user agent, and Instagram used an old Android Chrome login user agent while API calls used a different Instagram Android app user agent with `en_US` locale.
- Spotify login now uses `https://accounts.spotify.com/login` without a fixed `/en/` locale or custom webview user agent. Spotify API requests now send `Accept-Language` from `Locale.preferredLanguages` instead of hard-coded `en`.
- Spotify login now sets the post-login `continue` URL to `https://open.spotify.com/?nd=1` so the web player loads after login without relying on a navigation-cancel/rewrite fallback.
- Instagram login now uses a more current Android WebView user agent. Instagram API calls keep using the Instagram Android app user agent, because live simulator probes showed saved sessions return HTTP 400 `useragent mismatch` with the WebView user agent while the app user agent succeeds for both `current_user` and `reels_tray`. API calls still use a device-local `Accept-Language` header instead of hard-coded English locale hints.
- Instagram login no longer dismisses as soon as only a `sessionid` cookie appears. The WebView waits until all cookies required by the mobile API session are present (`sessionid`, `csrftoken`, `ds_user_id`, `mid`, `rur`, and `ig_did`) before saving and closing. This lets verification continue until the cookie set is complete without requiring navigation to the home screen.

### Spotify story preloading

- `UnifiedStoryViewer` now preloads adjacent Spotify stories' preview audio in the background by resolving the previous/next preview URLs, creating `AVURLAsset`s, and loading their durations. When the user navigates to a preloaded Spotify story in either direction, playback creates the `AVPlayerItem` from the warmed asset instead of starting from a cold URL fetch.

## 2026-05-13

### Farcaster reply notification discrepancy

- Live Hypersnap check for FID `1689` and cast `d03cc675be6caa0ae98df283a989d3341083d901` found `GET /v2/farcaster/cast/conversation` returns 4 direct replies with `parent_hash` equal to that cast and `parent_author.fid == 1689`.
- The same cast appears repeatedly in the first `GET /v2/farcaster/notifications?fid=1689&limit=50` page as `likes` target data, but none of the 4 direct replies appear as `reply` notifications. Given the reply timestamps are interleaved with first-page like timestamps, they would be expected in the current notifications response if parent-based reply lookup were active.
- Conclusion: Hypersnap can read the replies through the conversation path, but the deployed notifications endpoint is still omitting direct parent-based replies for this case.


### Feed and story feedback pass

- Pull-to-refresh now short-circuits while foreground/on-appear refresh is active and the refresh modifier is removed during active refresh state, preventing overlapping manual and automatic refreshes.
- `FeedViewModel` now runs a safe foreground-style auto refresh every 15 seconds. This reuses the existing automatic policy: X stays count-only through the source path, fetch-capable sources update cache/story content, and pending new notifications remain gated behind the `N New` affordance.
- Manual refresh now treats changed cached grouped notifications as new, not only brand-new IDs, when a non-X group gains actor IDs under the same stable group ID. X grouped notifications are excluded from this diff because the live X notifications endpoint can vary grouped actor metadata between consecutive fetches without an actual new notification.
- Notification detail no longer displays `Loading post details`; target hydration now shows only a compact loading indicator. Multiple target images render as a horizontal carousel instead of a vertical stack.
- Reply details render a `Parent Post` section when sources provide `parentTarget`. X reply entries populate this from in-memory parent tweets when X includes the parent in `globalObjects.tweets`; Farcaster stores parent hash/author metadata when Hypersnap provides it, but full parent text still depends on a future source response or targeted hydration.
- Story seen marking is delayed until after the full-screen presentation animation window completes, avoiding immediate seen-state changes before the modal visibly settles.
- Story entry behavior now depends on the tapped item: tapping an unread story opens only unread story-bar items, while tapping a seen story opens that user's story item/slides only.
- Instagram story rendering now decodes and displays lightweight rich metadata pills for `reel_mentions` and `story_link_stickers`, linking @mentions to Instagram profiles and story links to their target URLs.
- `UnifiedStoryViewer` preloads adjacent Instagram story images in addition to existing adjacent Spotify audio preloading.
- Spotify activity now carries the Spotify buddylist track context name and displays it as `From ...` below album metadata when available, surfacing playlist/context information for the song.

### Spotify login validation

- Spotify credential saves now require `SpotifyClient.validateAccount()` to resolve the authenticated username through the `profileAttributes` API. The previous fallback username (`"spotify"`) was removed because it could make incomplete credentials look valid.
- If username resolution fails after captured credentials are saved, the settings flow now deletes the saved Spotify credentials, clears Spotify account metadata, sets a service-error status, and shows an explicit retry message instead of treating the login as a successful connection.
- `SpotifyConnectionView` keeps the login button visible whenever no Spotify username is connected, including service-error states, so users can immediately retry after a failed credential capture/validation.

### Seen story navigation

- Tapping a seen story now opens the full seen-story set, starting at the tapped item, instead of limiting navigation to only that user's story item. Tapping an unread story continues to open only unread story-bar items.

### Story mention profile navigation

- Instagram story @mention pills now navigate to `ProfileDetailView` when the `reel_mentions` payload includes a user id. `UnifiedStoryViewer` receives the shared `FeedService` and wraps its content in a `NavigationStack` so mention taps use the same native profile detail path as notification detail People rows. Mentions without a user id remain non-navigating text pills.

### Auto refresh interval removal

- Removed the 15-second foreground auto-refresh loop from `FeedView`. The app still refreshes on foreground activation and supports pull-to-refresh, but no longer polls continuously while the feed is open.

### Spotify story progress behavior

- Removed the animated Spotify loading-progress pulse in `UnifiedStoryViewer`; loading now uses a stable gray progress track so the full-screen modal does not show a bouncing/pulsing loader during presentation.
- Spotify story slides now advance immediately when the preview `AVPlayerItem` reaches end-of-playback. The timer also permits `.finished` Spotify status so elapsed-duration fallback behavior remains consistent with Instagram story slides.

### Instagram music story duration

- Instagram story music stickers decode clip timing from `start_time_ms`/`end_time_ms` when present, with fallback duration fields from `music_asset_info` (`duration_in_ms`, `duration_ms`, `audio_asset_duration_ms`) for future audio handling.
- Instagram story timing remains media-driven: video slides use `video_duration` when available, while still-image slides stay at the default 5 seconds even when they have a music sticker. The app does not currently play a separate Instagram music audio asset; video audio continues to come from the story video stream itself.

### X tweet notification category

- Added `XNotificationCategory` with configurable Mentions, Replies, Reactions, and Tweets categories. `XAccountMetadata` now persists enabled X categories with a default of all categories enabled for existing stored accounts.
- `XConnectionView` now shows the same per-category notification toggles used by Farcaster and Instagram. `SettingsViewModel` persists X category toggle changes through `AccountMetadataStore`.
- X post/tweet notification elements such as `device_follow_tweet_notification_entry`, `user_tweeted`, `user_tweeted_entry`, `tweet_notification`, and `user_posted` normalize to the new `.post` `NotificationType`. The X source filters normalized notifications through the configured X category set before returning items to the feed.
- Live simulator credential testing against `GET /i/api/2/notifications/all.json` showed the missing ~21h tweet notification is a `notification` entry with `element: device_follow_tweet_notification_entry`, `url.title: Posts`, non-empty `fromUsers`, and an empty `targetTweets` array. The parser now treats these notification entries as supported even without a target tweet, rendering them from actor metadata and the notification `sortIndex` timestamp.

### Instagram story pagination

- Changed Instagram story loading so `reels_tray` still establishes the full ordered tray, but `/feed/reels_media/` is requested only for the first 15 active, unmuted reels initially. Spotify activity is fetched in the same initial story-bar pass, so the first page across providers is merged before display without fetching all Instagram media pages.
- Added lazy Instagram story pagination: when the user reaches the end of the currently loaded story row, `FeedViewModel.loadNextStoryBarPage()` requests the next 15 Instagram reels and appends them to the unified stories bar. Additional pages are Instagram-only because Spotify activity has no paginated story provider path in the current implementation.
- `NotificationDetailView` now hydrates X `.post` notifications on demand with `GET /i/api/2/notifications/device_follow.json`. The feed remains no-fanout; detail fetches the current device-follow timeline and renders each returned tweet as a separate native post card. Live probing confirmed the endpoint returns `globalObjects.tweets` plus tweet timeline entries; X's grouped notification entries can omit `targetTweets`, so detail intentionally shows the device-follow feed instead of trying to correlate a stale grouped notification to exact tweet IDs.
- Updated product/technical direction so foreground activation refresh full-fetches X notifications rather than count-only polling. The app accepts X's possible server-side read side effect for foreground/manual refresh because current feed content is preferred.
- Foreground activation cache writes now replace only the networks that successfully refreshed, instead of replacing the entire notification cache with the partial result set. This preserves cached X/Farcaster rows when another foreground source succeeds while X or Farcaster fails transiently.
- Manual refresh now returns the existing cached feed without surfacing a blocking refresh alert when every source fails but cached notifications are available. If the cache is empty and all sources fail, the alert is still shown so first-run/account problems remain visible.
- Manual refresh now also replaces only successfully refreshed networks that returned items, matching foreground refresh. Failed networks and networks that transiently return an empty result keep their existing cached notifications, so consecutive refreshes cannot clear the feed just because one source failed or returned no current entries.
- Root-caused the reproducible post-auto-refresh manual refresh alert to task cancellation, not API failures: simulator logs showed X, Farcaster, and Instagram all failing with `NSURLErrorDomain Code=-999 cancelled`. `FeedView` used a conditional `.refreshableIf(!isForegroundRefreshing && !isRefreshing)` modifier; when pull-to-refresh set `isRefreshing = true`, SwiftUI recomputed the view, removed the `.refreshable` modifier, and cancelled the in-flight refresh task. The fix keeps `.refreshable` attached permanently and relies on `FeedViewModel.refresh()` guards to ignore overlapping refresh attempts.

## 2026-05-14

### Spotify liked-song status in full-screen viewer

- Added `areEntitiesInLibrary` (hash `134337999233cc6fdd6b1e6dbf94841409f04a946c5c7b744b09ba0dfe5a85ed`) and `addToLibrary` (hash `7c5a69420e2bfae3da5cc4e14cbc8bb3f6090f80afc00ffc179177f19be3f33d`) Pathfinder GraphQL operations to `SpotifyClient` for checking and setting track library status.
- These queries require the initial browser login Bearer token (from `reason=init`) which carries library scope. Refreshed WebPlayer transport tokens do not have sufficient scope.
- Spotify login now captures the transport Bearer token plus `sp_dc`/`sp_t`/optional `sp_key`, then `SettingsViewModel` fetches `https://open.spotify.com/api/token?reason=init&productType=web-player` natively after account validation and stores the returned token and expiry as `initialBearerToken` / `initialBearerTokenExpiresAt` on `SpotifyCredentials`.
- `SpotifyCredentials` gained `initialBearerToken: String?`, `initialBearerTokenExpiresAt: Date?`, and optional `spKey` to preserve the init token separately from the regular transport token and retain all relevant Spotify session cookies.
- Live Pathfinder testing showed `areEntitiesInLibrary` returns `403 Forbidden` without browser-like `Origin`, `Referer`, and `User-Agent` headers even when the token is valid. The Spotify library check/add requests now send those headers.
- `UnifiedStoryViewer` checks liked status for each Spotify story slide via `SpotifyClient.isTrackSaved(trackId:)`. When saved, shows a "Saved" badge with checkmark. When not saved, shows a `+` button that calls `SpotifyClient.saveTrack(trackId:)` to add the track to the user's Liked Songs.
- The Spotify story save control is icon-only and sits to the right of the "Open in Spotify" button at the same 44-point height; saved tracks render as a white checkmark in a green circle.
- Live mutation testing confirmed `addToLibrary` and `removeFromLibrary` share hash `7c5a69420e2bfae3da5cc4e14cbc8bb3f6090f80afc00ffc179177f19be3f33d` and require variables shaped as `{ "libraryItemUris": ["spotify:track:..."] }`. The saved checkmark now calls `removeFromLibrary` so the control toggles liked-song state both ways.
- Refactored Spotify Pathfinder calls behind `SpotifyClient.makePathfinderRequest(...)` and `pathfinderBody(...)` so auth, app, content type, language, and browser-origin headers are defined in one place instead of repeated across profile, library check, add, and remove operations.
- Spotify liked-song save/remove toggles now trigger a light iOS impact haptic after the API call succeeds and the visible state changes. macOS remains a no-op.

## 2026-05-16

### Story composer text deletion

- Story composer text captions now show a bottom-center trash target while an iOS text caption is being dragged. Dropping the caption over the highlighted trash target removes it from local composer state.
- The implementation keeps the existing UIKit-backed drag overlay for smooth movement and reports drag center points back to SwiftUI only for trash affordance visibility/deletion.
- The trash affordance was moved fully inside the UIKit drag canvas after SwiftUI state updates during every pan caused visibly low-refresh dragging. SwiftUI is now notified only when a drag finishes and whether the caption should be deleted.
- Added a composer toolbar download button on iOS. It renders the current local story composition to a 1080x1920 image with the selected photo aspect-fit on black plus text caption overlays, then saves it to Photos with add-only photo library permission.
- Successful story image saves now give non-modal feedback by temporarily changing the download toolbar icon to a checkmark; alerts are reserved for failures or missing permission.
- The story composer empty state now hides as soon as any local element exists, including text-only compositions without a selected background image.
- The story composer canvas now always has a tappable background layer, so tapping outside a focused text element clears text focus even in text-only compositions where no image or empty-state view is visible.
- The composer save button is enabled for any local composition element, not only selected photos. Text-only stories render as white caption overlays on a black 1080x1920 canvas.
- Fixed a text-drag flash near the trash target by preventing drag-time UIKit layout passes from resetting the text view to its committed offset; active drags now only relayout the trash target.
- Added a background color toolbar control to the story composer. Tapping cycles through solid black/white/blue/purple and gradient sunset/ocean/graphite backgrounds; selected backgrounds count as composition elements and are included in saved 1080x1920 story renders.
- The background toolbar control now matches the other toolbar buttons: a translucent circular button containing a smaller live circular swatch of the selected solid or gradient background.
- Text captions now store a per-element scale and support pinch-to-resize on iOS through the existing UIKit overlay. Pan and pinch gestures can recognize simultaneously, resizing stays local to UIKit during the gesture, and the committed scale is included in saved story renders.
- Focused iOS text captions now attach a keyboard accessory toolbar with `BG`, `Font`, `Color`, and `Done` controls. The first three controls cycle per-caption text background, font style, and text color, and those style choices are included in saved story renders.
- The text editing accessory toolbar is always attached to focused iOS text views. Toolbar controls use current-value icons: background swatch, font-styled `A`, text-color swatch, and keyboard-dismiss icon.
- Text captions now visually recenter only the focused UIKit text view while editing, without mutating the caption's saved offset or moving other captions.
- Fixed the text accessory toolbar controls collapsing together by giving each custom toolbar button explicit dimensions, internal center constraints, and spacing.
- Removed the extra filled background from custom text accessory toolbar buttons so the controls render as single toolbar icons/swatches rather than buttons inside buttons.
- While a text caption is focused for editing, the composer now inserts a dim tint overlay between the canvas/background and text overlays, isolating the active editing text without hiding other captions.
- Text caption layer ordering now places non-focused captions below the dim overlay and the focused caption above it.
- Focused text captions now animate into and out of the temporary edit position while preserving their saved canvas offset.
- The focused-text dimming overlay now ignores safe areas so the tint covers the full story composer backdrop.
- Removed the ineffective Core Animation suppression around text overlay layout. The composer now ignores keyboard safe-area resizing so the canvas and unrelated text overlays do not shift with keyboard presentation.
- Focused text captions now use an upward edit-center offset, placing the editing text slightly above the canvas center while keeping the saved caption position unchanged.

### Instagram story posting

- Updated product/technical scope to include Instagram story posting now that the original notification-focused MVP is implemented.
- Added `InstagramClient.publishPhotoStory(jpegData:width:height:)`, using the researched two-step private API flow: raw JPEG upload to `/rupload_igphoto/{upload_name}` with `X-Instagram-Rupload-Params`, then signed `POST /api/v1/media/configure_to_story/` with upload id, dimensions, crop metadata, and account CSRF/device fields.

- Story composer now has a post toolbar action. It renders the current 1080x1920 composition to JPEG and posts it to the connected Instagram story; save-to-Photos remains a separate local action.
- The first stories-bar bubble now represents the user's own Instagram story entry when account metadata/profile lookup is available: it shows the user's profile picture and a bottom-right `+` affordance, and tapping it opens the composer.
- The composer post action is now exposed as a prominent bottom `Post Story` button so posting is discoverable; the toolbar download button remains the local save action.
- Own Instagram stories in `UnifiedStoryViewer` show a top-bar kebab menu with a destructive `Delete Story` action. The action presents a confirmation dialog, then calls `POST /api/v1/media/{mediaId}/delete/?media_type=PHOTO|VIDEO` through `InstagramClient.deleteStory` and refreshes story bar content after success.
- Live API debugging found story photo upload succeeds but story configure still fails: `POST /rupload_igphoto/{upload_name}` returns HTTP 200 with `status: ok`, while `POST /api/v1/media/configure_to_story/` returns HTTP 500 `We're sorry, but something went wrong during media publish. Please try again.` for the same upload. A regular feed `POST /api/v1/media/configure/` with the same uploaded image succeeded, so credentials, upload bytes, and basic write access are valid. The accidental feed test post was deleted successfully.

- Decompiled Instagram Android 429.0.0.32.70 under ignored `third-party/instagram-429-decompiled` for request comparison. The official story builder confirms `media/configure_to_story/`, photo upload through `rupload_igphoto`, `include_e2ee_mentioned_user_list=1`, `upload_id`, `original_width`, `original_height`, `original_media_type=1`, `client_shared_at`, `client_timestamp`, `source_type`, optional `audience`, and `configure_mode` values derived from `ShareType`.
- Curl retries using APK/reference-aligned variants all still returned the same HTTP 500: signed and unsigned bodies, `configure_mode` values `1`, `2`, `3`, `11`, and `13`, `audience=default`, `source_type` `3` and `4`, `supported_capabilities_new`, `device_id`, `original_media_type`, `original_width`/`original_height`, `width`/`height`, `device`, `extra`, cookie-jar propagation from upload to configure, and story-specific `share_type=stories` rupload params.
- `GET /api/v1/accounts/current_user/?edit=true` with the same session returned HTTP 200 but no obvious story creation capability flag beyond `reel_auto_archive=on`. The remaining likely blockers are an official-client session capability/header not captured by webview cookies, a changed server-side requirement not present in the old `instagram-private-api` flow, or account/session trust gating specific to story publishing.
- Captured and curl-verified Instagram's web story publish flow. It uploads to `https://i.instagram.com/rupload_igphoto/fb_uploader_{upload_id}` with web app id `1217981644879628`, `Content-Type: image/jpeg`, and `X-Instagram-Rupload-Params` containing only `media_type`, `upload_id`, `upload_media_height`, and `upload_media_width`. Configure then posts an unsigned form to `https://www.instagram.com/api/v1/media/configure_to_story/` with `caption`, `configure_mode=1`, `upload_id`, and `jazoest` (`"2" + sum(CSRF unicode scalar values)`) plus web headers (`X-Requested-With`, `X-CSRFToken`, `X-ASBD-ID`, `X-Web-Session-ID`, and `X-Instagram-AJAX`). This returned HTTP 200 and created a story with the simulator credentials; the test story was deleted successfully afterward.
- Updated `InstagramClient.publishPhotoStory` to use the verified web story publish flow while leaving mobile API reads, story fetches, seen marking, and deletes unchanged.
- The first stories-bar bubble now opens the user's own active Instagram story when one exists. `FeedViewModel` separates the authenticated user's reel into `ownInstagramStoryReel` so it is not duplicated in the regular unified story list; the small `+` affordance on that bubble still opens the composer.
- The own story bubble is now hidden until the initial unified story-bar fetch completes. `FeedViewModel.storyBarContentLoaded` gates `StoriesBar` rendering so the user's bubble appears atomically with fetched Instagram/Spotify story content instead of flashing alone during startup.
- The own-story kebab menu no longer advances the story. Top-bar interactions are ignored by the story navigation gesture, and tapping the kebab pauses the current Instagram story timer/video before showing the delete menu.
- The own story bubble now renders its story outline with the same logic as regular Instagram story bubbles: gradient ring for unseen own stories, gray ring for seen own stories, and only a subtle placeholder outline when there is no active own story.
- Replaced the own-story delete `Menu` with an explicit kebab button plus `confirmationDialog`, because SwiftUI `Menu` did not reliably expose its open state for pausing. The kebab now pauses before presenting actions, resumes on cancel/dismiss, and stays paused while delete confirmation is pending.
- Replaced the first-step system story action dialog with a compact in-view action menu anchored near the kebab. The story remains paused while this menu is open, resumes when tapping outside it, and only uses the system confirmation dialog for the destructive delete confirmation.
- The in-view story action menu now has explicit `Delete` and `Cancel` rows; `Cancel` dismisses the menu and resumes the paused story.
- The Instagram own-story/posting bubble is hidden when no Instagram account is connected. The unified stories bar can still render non-Instagram story content such as Spotify activity without showing an unusable posting entry.
- Live simulator tray analysis showed Instagram's `reels_tray` order is only weakly chronological while strongly separating unseen from seen reels. The story bar now takes Instagram's first 15 fetched reels, merges that prefix with Spotify listening stories in reverse chronological order, then appends the remaining Instagram reels in their original fetched order. This gives the visible head of the bar a chronological feel while preserving Instagram's algorithmic ordering for the long tail.
- Added story-bar feed modes for `All Stories`, `Instagram`, and `Spotify` as a masked vertical pager. All rows are rendered in a fixed-height vertical scroll area so the next/previous row is visible during vertical scrolling, but only one row is visible at rest. Each row keeps independent horizontal story scrolling and launches the story viewer scoped to that row.
- Deleting an Instagram story slide from `UnifiedStoryViewer` now removes that slide from the active viewer state immediately, so the progress tabs and visible content update without waiting for the feed/story-bar refresh. The own-story kebab menu now only contains `Delete`; cancellation happens in the system confirmation dialog.
- Fixed tapped-story/viewer mismatch for unseen stories. The viewer now preserves the tapped story row's newest-first user ordering and starts at the tapped user; only the slides inside unseen Instagram reels are reordered oldest-first for chronological playback.
- Lowered the Swift package iOS platform minimum from `.v26` to `.v18`; macOS remains `.v26`.
- Added `UIDesignRequiresCompatibility=false` to `Info.plist` so generated iOS app plists explicitly opt out of compatibility-mode design requirements.
- Diagnosed iOS 26 Liquid Glass disappearing after lowering the minimum iOS version: xtool's SwiftPM packer produced a Mach-O `LC_BUILD_VERSION` with both `minos 18.0` and `sdk 18.0`, so the app was not treated as linked against the iOS 26 SDK. Patched local xtool to pass explicit linker `-platform_version` flags using deployment target `18.0` and active SDK `26.1`; rebuilt app binaries now report `minos 18.0` and `sdk 26.1`.

X and Instagram WebView login credential extraction now folds cookies into a dictionary with later values replacing earlier duplicates.

The parent appears first, followed by a compact reply connector and the reply post, instead of showing separate `Post` and `Parent Post` sections.

### 2026-09-20 — Native Spotify login WebView recovery

- The reported simulator incident began with a WebKit JavaScriptCore optimizing-compiler crash; subsequent WebContent replacements crashed in `dyld_sim` while reading the shared-cache UUID, before application code ran. The native app remained running with a blank login sheet. Restarted the simulator without erasing app data to clear that failed runtime state.
- Replaced the native Spotify login sheet's fetch/XHR token-capture scripts with a WKHTTPCookieStore observer. Login completes from a valid `sp_dc`; optional `sp_t`/`sp_key` cookies are retained. Main-frame navigation to the full WebPlayer is canceled, avoiding its JavaScript execution after authentication. Selection rejects empty/expired cookies, foreign domains and cookies scoped only to Accounts; only root-path cookies for the token host are accepted.
- Cookie-only credentials have an expired access-token date, invoking the existing native token-renewal path before account validation. Spotify client endpoints and token algorithms were not changed. Added `scripts/spotify-web-client.py validate-cookie-login` to verify renewal and friend activity from selected cookies alone, returning only a redacted summary. The interrupted WebView had no selected session cookies, so authenticated validation of the new completion flow awaits a fresh user sign-in.
- Web-process termination and navigation failures now display a native Retry screen after checking whether usable login cookies already arrived. Retry reloads Accounts in the same ephemeral cookie store; dismissal removes the observer/delegate and stops loading. Completed/canceled attempts and stale retry callbacks cannot complete login again.
- Validation: Swift formatting, `stupid-app build`, simulator build/install/launch, Python syntax compilation, and all nine cookie/token regression tests passed. The sign-in page rendered successfully. A controlled stop of its isolated simulator WebContent process displayed the recovery message; tapping Retry restored the Spotify welcome form. Temporary session-check files were removed. The updated native app was left on Spotify sign-in for the remaining live check.

Probe results exposed only identity fields, response field names/status, or presence indicators; session values stayed in memory.

Instagram notification categories and story preferences are preserved. Added isolated request/credential regression tests for viewer-only resolution, incomplete viewer rejection, Spotify browser headers and client-token decoding, identity persistence, and retained identity during a profile-service failure.

Automatic repair populated both saved usernames; Settings displayed both handles, and the feed visibly rendered Instagram's own-account photo and username. Temporary probe scripts were removed; captures and build/test logs remain in ignored `logs/`.

### 2026-10-15 — Instagram story deletion via mobile REST

- Story deletion was broken: the web Relay `usePolarisStoriesV3DeleteMediaMutation` returned a `for (;;);` error payload (`1357001`, "Not Logged In") on both `/graphql/query` and `/api/graphql` despite valid bootstrap state, and web `POST www.instagram.com/api/v1/media/<id>/delete/` returned HTTP 400 `useragent mismatch`. Mobile `i.instagram.com` endpoints with cookie auth also returned a generic `status=fail`.
- Probe-validated replacement: `POST https://i.instagram.com/api/v1/media/<pk>_<ds_user_id>/delete/?media_type=PHOTO|VIDEO` with form body `media_id=<pk>_<ds_user_id>`, `Authorization: Bearer IGT:2:<base64({ds_user_id,sessionid})>`, `X-IG-App-ID: 567067343352427` and either the Instagram Android or the iPhone Safari user agent. Returns `{"did_delete":true,"status":"ok"}`; a follow-up `feed/user/<id>/story/` read with the same bearer auth confirmed removal. Validated against three freshly posted test stories, all removed.
- `scripts/instagram-web-client.py delete-story` now uses this request (accepts bare or owner-suffixed media IDs, `--video` for video stories) and `InstagramClient.deleteStory` mirrors it, failing loudly unless `did_delete` is true. Removed the unused delete mutation operation name from both.

### 2026-10-15 — Story viewer username truncation and per-user slide resume

- The story viewer top bar now limits the username (and timestamp subtitle) to one line with tail truncation so long names no longer wrap and push the bar layout around.
- `onChange(of: currentItemIndex)` used to reset `currentSlideIndex` to 0, which silently overrode `goBack()`'s jump to the previous user's last slide. Every navigation path already sets the slide index explicitly, so the reset was removed.
- `UnifiedStoryViewer` keeps an in-memory `lastSlideIndexByItemID` (keyed by `StoryBarItem.id`). Leaving a user records the slide being shown; returning via tap-back or swipe-back resumes that slide. Tap-back into a user not yet visited in this session falls back to their last slide; swipe-back falls back to slide 0.

### 2026-10-15 — Story composer caption scale range

- Caption pinch scaling is now clamped to 0.1–10× (previously 0.45–2.5×) via shared `minimumScale`/`maximumScale` constants. The caption hit-test area expands to at least 140pt around tiny captions so they remain pinchable and can be scaled back up.

### 2026-10-24 — Farcaster links open Quilcast

- Farcaster cast and profile links now open [Quilcast](https://github.com/backmeupplz/quilcast), a Farcaster web client backed by the same Hypersnap network the app reads from, instead of `farcaster.xyz`.
- `NotificationTargetURL` builds `https://quilcast.com/c/0x<hash>` (Quilcast's thread route accepts a 40-hex cast hash with or without `0x`); `ProfileDetailView` builds `https://quilcast.com/u/<username>` (Quilcast's profile route accepts a username or fid).
- Made the client configurable: Farcaster settings now has an "External Client" row (shown once an account is connected) that opens a picker with Farcaster, Quilcast, and Custom. Quilcast is the default. Custom shows cast and profile URL pattern fields using `{hash}` (`0x`-prefixed) and `{username}` placeholders.
- `FarcasterExternalClient` (in `AppModels.swift`) stores the choice and custom patterns in `UserDefaults` (`farcasterExternalClient`, `farcasterCustomCastURLPattern`, `farcasterCustomProfileURLPattern`) and builds the URLs for `NotificationTargetURL` and `ProfileDetailView`. A custom pattern that is missing its placeholder or a URL scheme produces no link, which hides the open action. Custom patterns may use any scheme, so a native client's URL scheme works.

### 2026-10-25 — Instagram story seen not persisting (investigation, unresolved)

- Reproduced live with simulator credentials: the web `PolarisAPIReelSeenMutation` (`/graphql/query`, numeric media `pk`, iPhone Safari UA) returns HTTP 200 `status=ok` with `data.xdt_mark_story_reel_seen: null` and no `errors`, and a follow-up mobile `POST i.instagram.com/api/v1/feed/reels_tray/` (IGT bearer auth) still reports the reel's `seen: 0`. The mutation no-ops silently. `InstagramClient.markStorySeen` discards the response and `InstagramNotificationSource.markReelAsSeen` uses `try?`, so the app never surfaced the failure.
- The probe's `stories-tray` and `current-user` commands are also broken: web `/api/v1/feed/reels_tray/` and `/api/v1/direct_v2/inbox/` now return HTML (404 / not-logged-in page). The mobile `i.instagram.com/api/v1/feed/reels_tray/` with IGT bearer auth (same as delete-story) returned the tray correctly.
- WARNING: a signed mobile `POST i.instagram.com/api/v2/media/seen/?reel=1&live_vod=0` (HMAC `signed_body`, `reels={"<pk>_<owner>":["<taken_at>_<seen_at>"]}`, IGT bearer auth, Android UA) returned 403 `login_required` (`logout_reason: 3`, `session_expired`), and the session was invalidated server-side immediately afterwards (subsequent bearer requests returned `logout_reason: 8`; web bootstrap no longer returned a `sessionid` cookie). Do not retry this request shape against a real session.

### 2026-10-25 — Instagram expired-session detection

- Instagram signals a dead web session by answering `GET /` with `302 → https://www.instagram.com/` plus `Set-Cookie: sessionid=…; expires=Thu, 01-Jan-1970 …; Max-Age=-…`. The native client sends cookies manually (`httpShouldHandleCookies = false`), so URLSession followed the redirect with the same dead cookie until it failed with too many redirects. That surfaced as a generic error, so accounts were stored as `serviceError` and the app never offered a reconnect.
- Probe first: `scripts/instagram-web-client.py` now fails loudly with "Instagram session expired" whenever a response (including a redirect) expires `sessionid`. Verified against the expired simulator session (`bootstrap` exits 1 with that message) and against a session-less request (no false positive).
- Native: `webDataRequest`/`webTextRequest` attach a per-task `InstagramSessionExpiryRedirectGuard` that refuses redirects expiring `sessionid`, and throw `SourceError.notConfigured` for any response that expires it. Existing handling then marks the account `invalidCredentials`. Covered by `testInstagramDetectsExpiredSessionCookie`.
- `SettingsViewModel` statuses previously reloaded only at init and when Settings appeared, so an account invalidated by a refresh did not badge the Settings button until relaunch. `ContentView` now reloads statuses after foreground, shortcut and pull-to-refresh refreshes; `FeedView` takes the refresh as an `onRefresh` closure instead of duplicating it.
- The Settings toolbar badge was passed to `FeedView` as a plain `Bool` read from the unobserved `SettingsViewModel` (`ObservableObject`), so it only changed when `ContentView` happened to re-render and stayed visible after reconnecting. A small `SettingsToolbarIcon` view now observes the view model directly. The dot is attached to the gear image (offset inward) so the iOS 26 glass toolbar container no longer clips it.
- Validation: swiftformat, `stupid-app build`, 17 `AccountIdentityTests`/`CookieHeaderParserTests` on the iOS simulator, and a simulator install where the expired session was stored as `invalidCredentials` and the Settings button showed the attention badge.

### 2026-10-25 — Instagram story seen: web mutation rejected (unresolved)

- The current web story viewer (`usePolarisStoriesV3MarkSeen` → `usePolarisStoriesV3SeenMutation`) uses `PolarisStoriesV3SeenMutation` (doc `26234228992942885` at time of writing), not `PolarisAPIReelSeenMutation` (which the native client uses and which is now only referenced by the older `PolarisAPIReelSeen` helper). Variables are identical: `reelId`, `reelMediaId` (media `pk`), `reelMediaOwnerId` (owner `pk`), `reelMediaTakenAt` (int), `viewSeenAt` (int seconds). Root field `xdt_mark_story_reel_seen` (`XDTMarkSeenResponse`, selects `__typename`), so a successful call returns a non-null object; `null` means failure. There is no web REST fallback.
- Live with a freshly reconnected session, both mutations return HTTP 200 `xdt_mark_story_reel_seen: null` without the Relay friendly-name fields, and `for (;;);` error `1357001` "Not Logged In" with `fb_api_req_friendly_name`/`X-FB-Friendly-Name`/`X-Root-Field-Name`. The tray `seen` stays 0. This matches the Oct 15 web delete-mutation failure.
- Ruled out (no change in result): either of the two distinct `DTSGInitialData` tokens on the homepage; adding the WebView-only cookies `datr`, `ps_l`, `ps_n`, `wd` (the app only keeps `sessionid`, `csrftoken`, `ds_user_id`, `mid`, `rur`, `ig_did`); adding Comet form fields `av`, `__d`, `__comet_req`, `__spin_r/b/t`, `__crn`, `dpr`, `__ccg`. The homepage and mobile-bearer tray reads agree on `seen`.
- After roughly a dozen mutation attempts in a short window, a read-only GraphQL query returned HTTP 400 `{"spam":true}`. Stopped testing; the session remained valid. Space out future experiments.
- Next step: capture the exact request a real logged-in browser sends when viewing a story (headers, form fields, cookie names) and diff it against the probe before trying more variants. Do not retry the signed mobile `api/v2/media/seen/` request (it invalidated a session).

### 2026-10-25 — Instagram story seen fixed (web actor id)

- Captured the real request from a logged-in desktop Chromium session viewing a story (Browser Control network capture; credential values redacted). The browser sends `PolarisStoriesV3SeenMutation` to `/api/graphql` with `fb_api_caller_class=RelayModern`, `fb_api_req_friendly_name`, `X-FB-Friendly-Name`, no `X-Root-Field-Name`, `__user=0` (the page's own `USER_ID`), and `av=<actorID>`, where `actorID` (also `NON_FACEBOOK_USER_ID`) is a 17-digit Instagram actor FBID distinct from `ds_user_id`. Its calls advanced the tray `seen` timestamp.
- Root cause: the probe and native client never sent `av` (the probe also replaced `__user=0` with `ds_user_id`). Without `av`, Relay mutations are rejected ("Not Logged In" with the friendly name, silent `null` root without it). One request with `av` from the page `actorID` and `__user=0` returned `xdt_mark_story_reel_seen: {"__typename":"XDTMarkSeenResponse"}`.
- Probe: `parse_web_state` records `page_user_id` and `actor_id`; `graphql_post` sends `__user` from the page and `av`; `story-seen` uses the V3 mutation with its friendly name on `/api/graphql` and exits non-zero unless the root field is non-null (the `api-reel-seen` fallback was removed). Verified live.
- Native: `InstagramWebState.actorID` is parsed from the homepage and sent as `av` on every GraphQL POST; `story-seen` maps to `PolarisStoriesV3SeenMutation` on `/api/graphql` with the friendly name; `markStorySeen` throws unless `xdt_mark_story_reel_seen` is an object; `markReelAsSeen` no longer swallows errors, and `StoryBarViewModel` reports failures through `errorMessage`. Covered by `testInstagramStorySeenSendsActorAndFailsWhenIgnored`.
- The missing `av` may also explain the Oct 15 "Not Logged In" from the web delete mutation and could matter for other Relay mutations (like/unlike, follow); not re-tested here.

### 2026-10-25 — Apron chat servers

- Added [Apron Chat Protocol](https://github.com/shazow/apron) (PROTOCOL.md v9) support. The spec is cloned into ignored `third-party/apron/` for reference. Users add any number of servers in Settings → Apron (URL, optional bearer token, optional requested display name); `wss://server.apron.chat/` is the placeholder example.
- Probe first: `scripts/apron-web-client.py` is a dependency-free WebSocket client (`hello`, `rooms`, `history`, `join`, `send`, `listen`). It redacts `token` fields and keeps any issued token in ignored `logs/apron-probe-token`. Verified read-only against `wss://server.apron.chat/`.
- Demo server behaviour (`apron-cloudflare-demo/9`): capabilities `history, edit, rooms, reactions, command, activity, embed:upload, status, ext`; `auth: [token, guest]`, `signup: [token]`, `ping: 45`. Guest auth issues no token, and each connection becomes a new `guest_NNNN` user. Guests are auto-joined to `general`, can read, and cannot post (`guest_posting: false`); a token comes from the demo web client (passkey sign-in or `/invite-bot`). A `~private` welcome notice without `room_id` arrives right after sign-in. `history` without `after` returns the newest page and includes `reactions`.
- Native: `ApronConnection` (`URLSessionWebSocketTask`) waits for the `server` frame, matches replies by request `id` (registered before sending, 15s timeout), forwards other frames as notifications, sends `{"method":"ping"}` at `server.ping`, and fails every pending request when the socket closes or the server sends a connection-level error. `ApronService` owns the server list (`account.apron.servers` in UserDefaults) and the per-server token (Keychain account `apron.<url>`, iCloud-synced like other credentials). Sign-in uses `token` when a token is saved and the server accepts it, otherwise `guest`; any `token` returned by `auth` replaces the saved one (§3.2.1). A `denied` sign-in marks the server invalid, which shows the red dot in Settings. There is no fallback from a rejected token to guest.
- Feed: `ApronNotificationSource` opens a short-lived connection per server, lists `joined` rooms, and reads each room's newest `history` page (10 changes). It emits one `.message` item per room for the latest non-deleted message from the last 24h ("<author> in <room>", or "mentioned you in" when `body.mentions` contains the viewer). `accountId` is the server URL, `target.id` is the room, and `parentTarget.text` is the room title. Servers without both `rooms` and `history` produce no feed items. There is no unread tracking because the demo server disables read cursors.
- Chat detail: tapping an Apron item opens `ApronChatView` instead of `NotificationDetailView`. `ApronChatViewModel` keeps a live connection while the view is visible. It installs snapshots by the greatest `log_id` (a tombstone wins a tie, §2.3), orders messages by `message_id`, pages earlier history with `before = first_log_id - 1`, joins the room when it isn't in `joined`, and sends `activity.read_message_id` when the server supports `activity`. The composer sends `message`. Text that starts with `/` goes as `command` when the server supports it, and `//` escapes a leading slash (§4.1). Server errors such as guest posting denial show inline. Markdown is rendered inline-only with `AttributedString`, so raw HTML is never rendered. Reactions, embeds, edits and avatars are not rendered yet.
- Badge `Resources/ApronBadge*.png` is downscaled from the Apron repo's `art/apron-square.png` (MIT).

### 2026-10-25 — Apron chat moved to UIKit; floating composer, attachments, avatars

- Benchmarked a SwiftUI (`ScrollView` + `LazyVStack`) chat against a UIKit one (`UICollectionView` list layout + diffable data source + self-sizing `UILabel` cells). Both used the same view model and the same cached Markdown parse. Setup: release build swapped into the simulator app (`stupid-app run --simulator` only builds debug), NoFeedSocial iOS 27.0 simulator, the local Bun reference server (`third-party/apron/servers/js-minimal`) seeded with 999 messages via `scripts/apron-web-client.py seed`, and the full log loaded before render. A temporary `CADisplayLink`/`thread_info`/`task_vm_info` monitor drove scripted flicks through `stupidsim`. The averages below cover the SwiftUI scroll runs (3) and one run of each of the others:

  | metric | SwiftUI | UIKit |
  |---|---|---|
  | first frame after publishing 1,000 messages | 190–313 ms | 99–150 ms |
  | scroll hitch time (ms per s) | 75–85 | 19–23 |
  | longest frame while scrolling | 315–481 ms | 175–217 ms |
  | main-thread CPU during 20 s of flicks | 6.3–7.2 s | 3.4–3.5 s |
  | footprint after scrolling | 102–116 MB | 83–87 MB |
  | live burst of 150 messages: hitch ms/s | 39 | 25 |

  These are simulator numbers, so they show the relative difference rather than device performance. UIKit was kept. The SwiftUI implementation, the implementation switch and the benchmark monitor were removed. `ApronChatView` (SwiftUI) now only hosts `ApronChatUIKitView` and owns the title and connection lifecycle.
- Composer: it floats over the list. There is a Liquid Glass (`UIGlassEffect`, iOS 26+; thin material before) `+` circle and an input pill. The list extends under it and its bottom inset tracks the composer's top edge (`keyboardLayoutGuide`). The send button appears only when there is text or an attachment. Connection and send errors show in a glass notice capsule above the input.
- Attachments: `+` opens a menu with Photo Library (`PHPickerViewController`, images re-encoded to JPEG ≤2048 px, GIFs kept) and Files (`UIDocumentPickerViewController`, copy). One staged attachment shows in the pill and is sent as an `upload` embed (§4.8.4). The `message`/`command` result's `embeds[0].write_url` gets an HTTP `PUT` with the file's MIME type. The button is disabled unless the server advertises `embed:upload`. The app rejects files over a limit the server sets in `ext.media.upload[mime].max_bytes` (the demo uses 5 MiB per image type). Messages now go out as `format: markdown`, matching the web client.
- Embeds in messages: an `og.image` from the chat server's host or an `ext.media.origins` host (demo: `media.apron.chat`) renders as an image sized from `og.image` width and height. A pending upload (no `url`) shows "Uploading…". Anything else is a card with its title, description or host, which opens `url` when it is http(s). This covers the §3.5.2 fallback for unknown kinds. Verified against a real upload on the demo server.
- Feed avatars: a message's `from` is a recorded object that usually omits `avatar`, so the source now lists joined rooms with `members: true` and takes avatars from the complete `users` objects (on the demo server, 9 of 26 users had one). `data:image/{png,jpeg,gif,webp}` avatars are accepted alongside http(s). The actor's `username` is the display name because feed rows bold `username`, and Apron has no handle besides the opaque `user_id`. Apron has no server or room icons: the `server` frame and room records have no image field, and the demo's `ext` has none either.
- Servers without `rooms` now produce a feed item from their default room (history without `room_id`), for example the minimal reference servers.
- Not verified end to end: the upload write path (`PUT` to `write_url`). The demo server only lets guests read, the Bun server has no `embed:upload`, and running the Go reference server (`apron-chat/apron-server-go`, which supports uploads and guest posting) was not permitted in this session. `scripts/apron-web-client.py upload` implements the same flow for when it can be run.

### 2026-10-25 — Apron chat bubbles (Telegram style)

- Messages render as bubbles. Your own messages are right-aligned on `systemBlue` with white text; others are left-aligned on `secondarySystemBackground`. A sender group is consecutive messages from one `user_id` less than 10 minutes apart. The sender's name (in a stable per-user color: the sum of the `user_id`'s Unicode scalars indexed into seven system colors, since `hashValue` is seeded per launch) heads a group, and a 34 pt avatar sits beside its last bubble. Cells are reconfigured when either their snapshot or their group position changes, because a new message can end the previous message's group.
- Avatars in the chat come from the `room_list` `members: true` users kept by `ApronChatViewModel` and fall back to the recorded `from`. Without an image they show initials on the user's color. Servers without `rooms` therefore only show initials.
- Time sits inline at the bottom-right. The body gets the time appended again in clear text, so the visible label overlaps the placeholder on the last line or wraps onto its own line. An empty `NSTextAttachment` spacer drew a visible line and was replaced. Messages with only embeds get a spacer row for the time.
- Replies show a quote inside the bubble: an accent bar and tint in the replied-to author's color (white on your own bubbles), the author's name, and one line of text. System identities (`~server`, `~room`, `~private`) render as centered footnote captions without a bubble.
- The composer's send button uses a fixed 36 pt square `UIButton.Configuration` (the stack had stretched the symbol into an oval), `systemBlue` to match outgoing bubbles, and the configuration's activity indicator while sending.
- Inline formatting: Markdown code spans render monospaced (92% of body size) on a highlight (`label` at 8% on incoming bubbles, white at 22% on your own). `@` mentions match the Appendix A.3 ID rule, which requires no letter or digit before the `@`, so `ada@example.com` is not a mention. They render in the link color (`.link`) at regular weight. On your own blue bubbles, where the text is white, they are underlined instead. They are never matched inside code spans. Links use `.link` rather than the app's monochrome tint. Plain-format messages keep backticks literal as §3.5.2 requires, though their `@` mentions are still colored.

### 2026-10-25 — Apron replies, reactions, jump-to-message

- Swipe to reply: a `UIPanGestureRecognizer` on each message cell begins only for a leftward, mostly horizontal drag (|vx| > 1.2·|vy|), so vertical scrolling and the navigation back swipe still work. The bubble and avatar follow the finger up to 64 pt, then rubber-band to at most 110 pt. A reply arrow grows in from the right edge. A medium `UIImpactFeedbackGenerator` fires once each time the drag crosses the threshold, and releasing past it starts the reply. The spring back (`UIViewPropertyAnimator`, damping 0.82, carrying the release velocity) is interruptible: a new pan stops the animator with `stopAnimation(true)` and continues from the bubble's current offset. Verified on the simulator, including a second grab during the spring.
- Replying: `ApronChatViewModel.replyingTo` shows a "Reply to …" bar in the composer pill with a cancel button. `send()` adds a bare `reply_to: {message_id}` (§3.5.4), which the local Bun server accepted and stored. Reply is also in the long-press menu.
- Reply quotes: when the target isn't loaded, the view model fetches it alone with `history` where `after` and `before` both equal its id (§4.3.1). The quote shows "Loading…", then the target, or "Message unavailable" when retention has dropped it (this happens on the Bun server, which keeps 1,000 records). Image-only targets read "Photo", other embed-only targets "Attachment".
- Jump to message: tapping a quote calls `jump(to:)`. A loaded target just scrolls to the center and flashes. Otherwise the view model replaces the loaded history with a window of `history(before: id, limit: 30)` plus `history(after: id + 1, limit: 30)`, without loading what lies between. It sets `hasLaterMessages` from the forward page's `more`, and applies `more` per direction, because a forward page's `more` means newer messages. While in a window, live messages beyond it aren't installed (edits to loaded messages are); they arrive when paging forward. A "Load Newer Messages" row, plus automatic paging within 400 pt of the bottom, calls `loadLater()`. On catching up it reads one extra page for anything that arrived during the last request. Verified by jumping about 540 messages back on the local server.
- A floating glass chevron button appears when scrolled more than 80 pt from the bottom or while viewing a window. In a window it reloads the newest page (`jumpToLatest()`); otherwise it scrolls to the bottom.
- Reactions: `history` pages and live `reactions` notifications update the per-user state messageId → userId → (log_id, emojis). The greatest `log_id` wins, duplicates collapse, and records from other rooms are ignored. Totals are sorted by count, then by first appearance. Chips sit inside the bubble, up to 5 plus "+N", leaving room for the time. Your own reactions are blue (white with blue text on your blue bubbles); others are neutral. Tapping a chip toggles your reaction by sending your complete new set with `reactions` (§4.7). The long-press menu has a 4-emoji quick row (`preferredElementSize = .small`), Reply, More Reactions (a sheet with a 36-emoji grid), and Copy. Reaction controls appear only when the server advertises `reactions`. The read path was verified against the demo server's history. The write (`reactions` request) has not been verified live, because demo guests are read-only and the Bun server has no `reactions`; `scripts/apron-web-client.py react` implements it for when it can be run.
- The long-press highlight and dismissal use `UITargetedPreview` on the bubble with a rounded `visiblePath`, so only the bubble lifts.
- The navigation bar background is hidden (`toolbarBackground(.hidden)`), so messages scroll beneath it. The principal item is a capsule with the room title and server host, using `glassEffect` on iOS 26 and `.regularMaterial` before it.
- Reaction chips were enlarged: a 20 pt emoji, a semibold subheadline count, 5/10 pt insets and 8 pt spacing. Tapping a quote whose original is unavailable does nothing, instead of reporting an error.
- History pages automatically. There are no "Load Earlier/Newer" rows: within one screen height of the top, `loadEarlier()` runs (the existing offset preservation keeps the view steady), and within one screen of the bottom of an older window, `loadLater()` runs. A small spinner under the title shows while earlier history loads. Verified by flicking up through several pages on the local server.
- Top bar: the chat host view ignores the top and bottom safe areas, so the list runs full-bleed under the transparent navigation bar. Messages soften under the bar with the iOS 26 `topEdgeEffect.style = .soft`, UIKit's equivalent of `.scrollEdgeEffectStyle(.soft, for: .top)`. This replaced a custom gradient-masked blur, at the user's request.
- The chat list hides its vertical scroll indicator.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron unread state and "New Messages"

- Investigation: Apron's read cursor is `activity.read_message_id` (§4.6). Servers MAY keep each user's latest cursor per room and send it after `room_list` lists the room. The Go reference server does, and persists cursors across restarts. The demo server says it doesn't (`ext.settings.read_cursors: false`) and sent no `activity` after `room_list`. A server-side unread count exists only in push payloads (§4.9.3), which would need a backend. Server cursors also can't help guests, who are a new `user_id` on every connection.
- `ApronReadStore` holds a cursor per server and room. If `ApronService.serverTracksCursors(session)` holds (the server has `activity`, doesn't set `ext.settings.read_cursors: false`, and the user signed in with a token), the server is the source of truth. Its cursors are captured from our own `activity` frames after `room_list` (during feed refresh and in the chat) and cached even when they move backward. Otherwise the cursor is stored on the device and in iCloud Key-Value storage (`com.stupid.apron.readcursors`), merged per room with `max` like `StorySeenSync`. Every `user_id` the user has had on a server is remembered, so their own earlier guest messages never count as unread. Removing a server clears its cursors.
- A room never read before gets a cursor at its newest message the first time it is seen, so adding a server doesn't flood the feed.
- Feed: every room with a message in the last 24h still appears. The refresh reads a 50-change page per room and stores `unreadCount` on the item: messages after the cursor that aren't deleted, system or your own, capped at 50 and shown as "50+". The count is a new `NotificationItem` field and a `CachedNotification` property with a `nil` default. The row shows a blue count capsule and blue timestamp. The badge clears as soon as the cursor reaches the item's latest message, without a refresh: `FeedView` watches `ApronReadStore.didChangeNotification`. Partly read rooms keep the refresh's count until the next refresh.
- Chat: the cursor at open marks where a "New Messages" divider goes, above the first unread message from someone else. It stays for the whole visit. The room opens with the divider at the top, or at the newest message when nothing is unread. When the first unread lies before the newest page, the room opens on a window of `history(before: cursor, limit: 10)` and `history(after: cursor + 1, limit: 50)`, with newer pages loading as you scroll, instead of paging through everything between. Messages are marked read only when fully on screen while the chat is visible and the app is active, plus when you send. `activity.read_message_id` goes to servers with `activity` 600 ms after the cursor last moved, only when it advances. The old behavior, which marked the newest loaded message read on open, was removed. The jump-to-latest button shows a blue badge with the number of loaded unread messages below.
- Verified on the local Bun server (device/iCloud path): 5, then 30 unread showed in the feed; the divider and its position were correct; the arrow badge counted down (25 after the first screen); and the feed badge cleared after reading. The server-as-truth path is untested, because it needs a token on a server that keeps cursors, such as the Go server.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron realtime feed, message cache, chat polish

- Realtime feed: `ApronLiveFeed` keeps one connection per saved server while the app is active. It starts at launch and on `scenePhase == .active`, stops in the background, and follows server additions and removals through `serversDidChangeNotification`. A logged `message` or a `room_update` rebuilds that server's items on the open connection, debounced 800 ms, reusing `ApronNotificationSource.items(in:)`. The rebuild replaces only that server's cached items (`NotificationCacheStore.replaceAccount`) and posts `didUpdateNotification`, after which `AppContainer` reloads the feed from the cache. The user's own `activity` read cursors update `ApronReadStore` when the server is the source of truth. Dropped connections reconnect with backoff from 1 s up to 60 s; a `denied` sign-in stops the loop. Verified: a probe post changed the feed row to the new message with "1 unread" within about 3 s, without a refresh.
- Message cache: `ApronMessageCache` writes the newest 50 snapshots plus their authors' user objects per room to `Caches/apron-messages/<sha256>.json`, on this device only. Saves happen 1 s after changes while connected and on leaving the room, unless an older window is showing. Opening a room shows the cache before connecting, with the divider placed from the known cursor, and history merges in by `log_id`. If the newest history page has `more` and starts after the cached messages, the cached ones are dropped so no hole shows. Verified by pausing the Bun server with `SIGSTOP`: the room rendered from the cache alone.
- The initial scroll waits for real bounds, because cached messages are applied before the first layout. Bottom scrolls repeat scroll → layout → measure (up to 4 passes) until stable, because self-sizing cells start at estimated heights. Opening at the divider uses two passes.
- Pinned-to-bottom: `isPinnedToBottom` is set when the room opens at the bottom (or at a divider that is also the bottom) and by jump-to-latest. Only the user's own dragging or deceleration changes it, and jumping to a message clears it. While pinned, new messages, reaction and quote growth, inset changes and bounds changes re-anchor to the bottom, so growth pushes content up rather than under the composer. These moves animate with a 0.3 s ease-out to the measured bottom, then settle without animation if real cell heights differ.
- The bubble has a `.fittingSizeLevel` width-0 constraint. Its width constraints are only maximums, so without this a short message could keep a wider size from an earlier layout pass (seen: "below 2" laid out at 290 pt).
- The unread divider is now a 1 pt blue hairline on each side of a blue "New Messages" label. The jump-button badge is a capsule sized by a padded label, which centers the count (the old version padded the text with spaces in a fixed-size label).
- Own messages are decided by `ApronChatViewModel.isOwn`: the current `you` or any `user_id` the user has had on the server (`ApronReadStore.ownUserIds`, loaded when the view model is created). Before, cached messages rendered before sign-in, with `you` still nil, showed as incoming and then jumped to the outgoing side once the connection signed in. Messages from the user's earlier guest identities now also render as their own.

### 2026-10-26 — One shared Apron connection per server

- The demo server answered "connection limit reached for this account" for a token user. The app had been opening up to three sockets per server: the live feed, each refresh, and each open chat. Servers may cap connections per account (the demo runs on a free-tier Cloudflare Worker).
- `ApronService.session(serverURL:)` now returns the app's single signed-in connection per server. It is reused while open, concurrent callers share one pending sign-in, and it reopens on demand. `ApronConnection` supports any number of `observe(notifications:onClose:)` observers, each removed with its token, in place of the single `onNotification`/`onClose`. The feed source, the live feed and the chat only observe the shared session. Leaving a chat or finishing a refresh removes the observer; it never closes the socket. `closeSessions` closes connections when the app goes to the background (through `ApronLiveFeed.stop()`), when a server is removed, or after a token change.
- The chat rejoins the shared connection on `didBecomeActiveNotification`. History loads after a reconnect only merge what arrived meanwhile; the divider and opening window are for the first load of a visit. Before this, a reconnect re-ran "open at first unread" and scrolled away from the bottom.
- Verified with `lsof` on the local Bun server: one established connection from the app with the feed only, with a chat open, and after leaving and refreshing. Live messages still reached the open chat, and after a background round-trip the chat reconnected and stayed pinned to the bottom with the new message.
- Bubble width is now computed in `ApronMessageCell.configure` instead of left to Auto Layout. It is the widest of: the name; the body measured with `boundingRect` at the maximum content width; the reply quote's one-line natural width; embeds and reaction chips (compressed fitting size, plus 40 pt left for the time under chips); and the time alone when there is no text. That width is capped at the maximum (80% of the row, minus the avatar column or own-side gutter) and applied as a priority-999 width constraint. The earlier `.fittingSizeLevel` hug was not enough: newly inserted cells, first measured at estimated size, still laid out at the maximum (seen: "back from background" at 290 pt, now 213 pt).

### 2026-10-26 — Apron chat feedback: links, opening position, typing, reactions

- Links in messages open on tap. `ApronAttributedText` stores each link's URL under a custom `.apronLink` attribute, rather than `.link`, which `UILabel` would restyle. It covers Markdown links and bare URLs, which `NSDataDetector` finds in plain text; code spans are skipped. The body is an `ApronLinkLabel` that lays its text out with TextKit to find the character under a tap. Its tap recognizer begins only on a link, so long-press menus and swipe-to-reply still work. Only `http`, `https` and `mailto` open, because message text is untrusted. Verified on the simulator: tapping a GitHub link in the demo's `general` opened Safari.
- Opening at "New Messages": the room scrolled to the divider using the cached first frame. History, reactions and reply quotes then arrived and grew cells above the divider, so the view drifted (seen opening about 25 messages short of the divider, with 36 unread). This was also the layout shift after opening. The controller now keeps `isAnchoredToDivider` while the room opens. Snapshot applies, reaction and quote updates, and layout passes all re-scroll the divider to the top, the same way `isPinnedToBottom` follows the bottom. Dragging, jumping to a message, jump-to-latest and sending all clear it. When everything fits on screen, the divider is also the bottom, so the list becomes pinned instead.
- Reactions are cached: `ApronMessageCache.Entry.reactions` holds one record for each user on each cached message, keeping that set's `log_id` so the §4.7 merge stays correct. It is optional so caches written before still decode. Bubbles render with their chips from the first frame.
- Reconnects: when the app goes to the background, the shared connection closes without an error. That no longer shows the "Disconnected / Reconnect" notice, whose height changed the composer and list inset; the state returns to connecting until the chat rejoins. A reconnect used to reload the newest page and apply `dropCacheBehindGap`. That dropped every loaded message older than the page, including the divider, and moved the view. Now a reconnect reads forward from the last loaded `log_id`, and `more` turns the rest into forward paging, as in an older window. The gap check runs only for the cache on first load. Verified: open at the divider, background, foreground, same position and unread count.
- Typing (§4.6): probe first. `scripts/apron-web-client.py typing` sends `activity.typing` and then `0`. On the demo server, a token user's typing is broadcast to the room with `from`, `typing` is not echoed to the sender, and a guest's typing is not broadcast at all. The chat sends `typing: 8` when the draft becomes non-empty, refreshes it every 5 s while typing, and sends `typing: 0` when the draft empties, before sending, and when leaving the room. Commands don't count. Other users' `typing` shows "Name is typing…" (or "A and B", or "A and N others") in a small floating glass capsule above the composer. It floats so that it never changes the list's insets. An indication ends when it expires, when the server sends `0`, or when a message from that user arrives. Verified: the app's typing and its stop on leaving reached a probe connection. The receiving UI was not verified live: guests' typing isn't broadcast on the demo, and the only token available is the user's own.
- Reaction chips no longer wrap. The bubble width measured the chip row with a compressed fitting size, which let a chip's title wrap ("👋" over "2", seen after toggling a reaction). Chips are now single-line with required compression resistance, and the row is measured from the chips' intrinsic widths.
- Feed rows: the plain-style row button hit-tested only drawn content, so taps in the gaps of a row missed. The row label now fills the width with a rectangular `contentShape`.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron link label blocked scrolling

- In build 138, a scroll that started on message text sometimes didn't scroll. `ApronLinkLabel` overrode `UIView.gestureRecognizerShouldBegin`, which UIKit also calls on the touched view for its ancestors' recognizers, including the collection view's pan (UISlider uses the same hook to stop scrolling). It returned `false` whenever the touch wasn't on a link. The override now gates only the label's own link tap and defers to `super` for every other recognizer. Verified on the simulator: a drag starting on bubble text scrolls the list, and tapping a link still opens Safari.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron own reactions highlighted from the cache

- On a cold load, chips with your reactions first rendered neutral and turned blue once the connection signed in. "Includes you" came from `yourEmojis`, which needs `you`. Logging reaction updates on a cold open showed the cached reactions applied before the first render, plus three updates after it, two of them on visible messages, which were exactly the chips with your reaction. `includesYou` is now decided from every identity the user has had on the server (`ApronReadStore.ownUserIds`, plus `you`), the rule `isOwn` already uses for messages, so it is known from the first frame. Toggling still sends the set of the current identity. Afterwards only one update arrived after the first render, on an off-screen message whose reactions weren't cached. A room's first open after upgrading from a build before 138 has no cached reactions, and neither does a room that has only been opened on an older window (the cache only saves the newest messages), so those can still fill in once.
