### 2026-10-26 — Apron reaction chip long press lists who reacted

- Long-pressing a reaction chip opens a context menu on the chip itself, through its own `UIContextMenuInteraction`, which takes precedence over the bubble's menu. The chip lifts alone with a capsule `visiblePath`, the same targeted-preview treatment the bubble gets. The menu lists only the users who reacted with the pressed emoji, oldest first by their set's `log_id`, with "You" for any of the user's identities. There is no header, because the lifted chip shows the emoji. Each row's subtitle is the time of that user's reaction set ("Today at 03:39", relative medium date plus short time). Apron logs a user's whole emoji set on a message as one record (§4.7), so this is when they last changed their reactions there, not per emoji. Rows show the reactor's avatar when it is already in `StoryImageCache`, otherwise initials on their user color. The rows have no action.
- `ApronChatViewModel.reactors(of:on:)` names reactors from the room's kept user objects, falling back to the recorded `from` of their reaction set (§3.3.1). Those `from` objects are now kept, so reactors who aren't listed members still have a name. The reaction cache writes them as well.
- Verified on the simulator against the demo's `general`: long-pressing "🔥 2" listed "You, Today at 03:39" and "shazow, Today at 03:55" over the lifted chip. Dismissing left the reaction unchanged, and long-pressing the bubble away from a chip still showed Reply, More Reactions and Copy.
- Your own row first showed initials even though your demo account has an avatar (`media.apron.chat/a/…`, seen with `scripts/apron-web-client.py rooms --members`). The menu only draws avatars already in `StoryImageCache`, and the chat loads avatars only beside incoming bubbles, which never includes yours. When a cell with reactions is configured, the chat now fetches its reactors' avatars into the cache, deduplicated while in flight. The message cache also keeps the user objects of the cached messages' reactors, not only their authors, so the avatar URLs are known before the connection is up. Verified: your row shows your avatar.

### 2026-10-26 — Feed scroll stutter during refresh

- Diagnosis: a Time Profiler trace (`xctrace record --template 'Time Profiler' --device <sim> --attach <pid>`, exported with `xctrace export` and aggregated per main-thread sample) of a pull to refresh while scrolling on the simulator. The main thread was fully busy for about 1 s right after the pull. In that window, `GitHubActivityParser.parse` ran twice (about 364 ms for the notifications source and about 363 ms for the stories bar, on the same For You page), and `instagramHomepageTray(html:)` took about 359 ms. Regex matching and `String.index(_:offsetBy:)` over whole HTML pages ran on the main actor, because the sources and clients are `@MainActor`, so parsing resumed there after the network await. Other costs were small: the SwiftData cache rewrite took about 20 ms. `networkBadgeImage(named:)` read a PNG from disk with `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` each time a row's body was evaluated, 16–18 ms over the trace.
- Fixes:
  - `GitHubActivityParser.parseConcurrently` and `instagramHomepageTrayConcurrently` are `@concurrent` wrappers, so the parsing runs on the concurrent pool.
  - `GitHubForYouFeedLoader` (shared by `GitHubNotificationSource` and `GitHubActivitySource`, built in `AppContainer`) fetches and parses the For You page once. Concurrent callers with the same credentials share the in-flight request. A result is reused for 10 s, so the stories bar starting just after the feed doesn't fetch again. Each source still persists any refreshed session cookies from the shared response. Settings' connection checks still call `GitHubClient` and the parser directly.
  - Badge images are cached after the first load, using the same 1x file, so the inline badge size doesn't change.
- Verified with the same trace after the change: parser time on the main thread went from about 1,086 ms to 0, with 424 ms (GitHub, parsed once) and 433 ms (Instagram) on background threads. The busiest main-thread 250 ms window went from 250 ms (fully blocked for about 1 s) to 121 ms. The refresh log shows `story-github-activity` and `manual-source-github` finishing together (2.07 s and 2.09 s), from the one shared request. These are simulator measurements.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron room members

- Tapping the title pill in an Apron chat opens `ApronMembersView`, a plain list of the room's members: you first, then by name, with avatar or initials, and roles as a subtitle. Checked with `scripts/apron-web-client.py rooms --members` against the demo server: each room in `room_list` carries `members` as `{user_id}` stubs (29 in General, no `member_count`), and the result's `users` has the full objects (§4.2.1).
- `ApronRoom` now decodes `members` and `member_count`. `ApronChatViewModel` starts the member set from the chat's existing `room_list members: true` call, or from `room_update.joined` when opening the room joins it. Live `room_update.memberships` for the room then add or remove users (§4.2.2). Membership records in history are not applied. With a truncated list, the footer shows "Showing N of M members", and the server's `member_count` is not adjusted for live changes.
- `members` stays `nil` on servers without `rooms`. The screen then shows a "No Member List" state once connected, and a spinner before that. The title stays tappable while connecting.
- `ApronUserColor` is now module-internal so the SwiftUI rows use the same initials colors as the chat.
- Online indicators: `ApronUser` decodes `status` (§4.5), and `ApronPresence` maps it to online, idle, do-not-disturb, offline, or an unknown value kept as written; `nil` or `""` mean no known status. The member list splits into "Online" (online, idle and dnd) and "Offline" when any member has a known status, and stays one section otherwise. Dots are green for online, yellow for idle and red for dnd. Idle, dnd and unknown values are also written in the subtitle.
- Statuses come from the complete `users` objects of the chat's `room_list` (the demo had 3 online and 26 offline). The chat merges live `user` notifications (`new`) into its kept objects (§3.3.1), and `scripts/apron-web-client.py listen` shows these frames carrying just `user_id` and `status`. The sign-in `user` burst (§4.5.4) goes out before the chat attaches its observer, which is why the `room_list` statuses are the starting point. Your own entry follows `you.status` while connected (`dnd` → dnd, `invisible`/`""` → offline, otherwise online), because the server only sends you your own setting.
- Apron has no last-seen time. The protocol has only the current `status`, and the demo server's user objects have no time field (`ext` holds only `bio`).

### 2026-10-26 — Apron typing rate and drafts

- The demo server answered typing with "typing updates are limited to 10 per minute". The chat re-sent `typing: 8` every 5 seconds while you typed (12 a minute). It also sent `typing: 0` whenever the draft emptied and on every send. Typing now follows apron-web (`apron-chat/apron-web`, `sendTyping` in `src/lib/protocol/client.ts` and `composerInput` in `src/routes/+page.svelte`). It announces `typing: 15`, refreshes at most every 12 s, and sends `typing: 0` after a 5-second pause in input. A sent message only clears the local state, because the message ends the indication for others (§4.6). A command still sends an explicit stop. A stop is skipped when the indication would lapse within 2 s anyway.
- The app also keeps a budget of 7 typing frames per rolling minute per server, shared by its rooms; apron-web has none. Frames beyond it are dropped. A dropped stop just lets the 15 s indication lapse.
- `scripts/apron-web-client.py typing-rate` sends a burst of typing frames (`--count`, `--interval`, `--stops`) and prints the frames that come back. As a guest on the demo server, 14 frames 0.5 s apart drew no reply. The limit seems to apply only to accounts. Signing the probe in with the simulator's saved token failed ("Frontend origin is not configured for passkeys"), so the limit hasn't been reproduced with the probe.
- Drafts: `ApronDraftStore` keeps unsent composer text per server and room in `UserDefaults` on the device (`apronDrafts`). `ApronChatViewModel.draft` loads it at init and saves on every change. An empty or whitespace-only draft is removed, so sending clears it. Removing a server removes its drafts. Replies and attachments being composed are not kept.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron profiles

- `ApronProfileView` shows one user: avatar with presence dot, name, `@user_id` (selectable, middle-truncated), status in words, roles and bio. It opens from a member row, and from a sender's avatar in the chat. The cell's avatar has a tap recognizer that calls `Actions.showProfile`, and the controller hands `message.from` to `ApronChatView`, which pushes it with `navigationDestination(item:)`. The profile resolves the kept current object through `ApronChatViewModel.currentUser(_:)`, so live `user` changes show.
- The bio is `ext.bio`, as in apron-web (`bioOf` in `src/lib/protocol/client-views.ts`). The demo server sets it for some users (for example a URL). `ApronUser` now has hand-written `Codable`: `ext` is free-form, so only `bio` is read, and an odd value never drops the user. Only `bio` is written back under `ext` in the message cache. Bio text stays selectable. Its `http`/`https` URLs, found with `NSDataDetector` as in message text, are tappable links in the link color.
- `ApronUserAvatar` (avatar or initials, plus the presence dot) is shared by the profile and the member rows. The members screen is now pushed through `navigationDestination(isPresented:)` instead of a `NavigationLink`. While it or a profile is on top, the chat's `onDisappear` keeps the connection observer, so those screens get live statuses. Previously, pushing the members screen disconnected the chat until it reappeared.
- Roles show as tags beside the name, on the profile and in member rows, instead of a Roles section and the row subtitle. `ApronRoleTags` uses the style of the "You follow each other" tag in `ProfileDetailView` (caption, medium weight, secondary, `secondary.opacity(0.1)` capsule). Member row subtitles now only name statuses that the dot doesn't explain (idle, dnd, unknown values).
- Offline users get a grey presence dot, on the profile and in member rows. On the profile the dot is a fifth of the avatar (about 19 pt); in rows it is a third. Each dot is centered on the avatar's edge at 45°.
- The profile writes the status in words only for idle, dnd and unknown values. Online and offline are left to the dot, as in member rows.
- Inline `@user_id` mentions in message text open that user's profile. `highlightMentions` tags each match with `.apronMention` (the ID without `@`), and `ApronLinkLabel` reports a tap on it through `Actions.showMentionedUser`. The profile is pushed with a bare `ApronUser(userId:)` and filled from the kept object. A link wins over a mention inside it (`https://example.com/@bob`). Mentions are linked by their text, like their highlight, not only when listed in `body.mentions`.
- Mentions show the user's name (`@stephancill` for `@stephancill_2586`), in message bodies and reply quotes. `ApronChatViewModel.mentionNames` maps `user_id` to name from the kept user objects. It leaves out names that another known user shares, so those mentions keep `@user_id`, as §3.3.1 requires. Unknown users keep `@user_id` too. A mention inside a link keeps its text. The tap target is still the `user_id`. When the map changes (such as when `room_list` arrives after cached messages render), the controller clears its attributed-text cache and reconfigures messages that contain `@`, directly or in their quote. The mention regex now lives in `ApronMention.pattern` in the core module, shared by both paths.
- Your own entry shows a "you" tag before the role tags (`ApronUserTags`) instead of "(You)" after the name, on the profile and in member rows.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron images, composer edge, mention autocomplete

- Tapping an uploaded image (`kind: "upload"` with an allowed `og.image`) opens `ApronImageViewerController`, presented over full screen with a cross-dissolve. It supports pinch and double-tap zoom (1–4×), tap to show or hide the close and save buttons, and a vertical swipe to dismiss. The swipe works when the image isn't zoomed in; it dismisses past 120 pt or 800 pt/s and springs back otherwise. Link-preview images still open their page. Save downloads the original bytes (so GIFs and full resolution survive) and adds them with `PHAssetCreationRequest` after `.addOnly` authorization. `NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription` now mentions chat images. The `performChanges` block lives in a `nonisolated static` helper: written inside the controller, it inherited main-actor isolation and crashed with `_dispatch_assert_queue_fail` when Photos ran it on its own queue.
- On iOS 26, the list has `bottomEdgeEffect.style = .soft`, and the composer has a `UIScrollEdgeElementContainerInteraction` (`edge: .bottom`). Messages now soften behind the floating composer as they do under the navigation bar.
- Image messages are laid out like Telegram. The bubble's single stack is now three sections: header (name, reply quote), media (uploaded images, 2 pt inset), and content (text, other embeds, reactions), each with its own margins. Images take the bubble's 16 pt corners where they touch its edge and have square corners against the header or the caption. The images set the bubble width (264 pt landscape; portrait sized by height up to 340 pt, at least 200 pt wide), and the caption wraps inside it. On an image with no caption or reactions, the time sits on a dark pill over the image.
- `@` autocomplete: when the caret is in an `@query` word (ID characters only), up to 5 suggestions appear in a glass card above the composer. Each shows an avatar, name and `@user_id`. Members whose name or `user_id` starts with the query come first, then names with a word that does. An empty query lists online members first, and you aren't offered. Picking one replaces the word with `@user_id `. The card hides the typing capsule while it is up. Sending now fills `body.mentions` with the known users written as `@user_id` in the text (§3.5.5). Before this, the app sent no `mentions`, so servers didn't treat its mentions as mentions.
- Local testing: `scripts/apron-web-client.py send` gained `--image-url` and `--image-size`. They attach a finished upload embed with an `og.image`, which the js-minimal server passes through. With the images served from `127.0.0.1` (the Bench server's host), the app shows them as uploads.
- The simulator's pasteboard service (`pasted`) once hung after `simctl privacy grant`. `UITextView.becomeFirstResponder` then blocked in `_supportsImagePaste` → `PBServerConnection`, and taps on the composer froze the app. Killing `pasted` didn't help; rebooting the simulator did.

### 2026-10-26 — Instagram checkpoint and logged-out responses require reconnect

- A session under an Instagram security checkpoint still loads the homepage (with the user's `actorID`) and expires no cookie, so the existing expired-session check missed it. API calls fail instead: `POST /api/v1/news/inbox/` answers HTTP 400 `{"message":"checkpoint_required",…}`, and `GET /api/v1/direct_v2/inbox/` answers HTTP 404 with a page rendered for a logged-out visitor (`<html class="… not-logged-in …">`). Both were surfaced as service errors, so the account never asked for a reconnect.
- Probe first: `scripts/instagram-web-client.py` now exits with "Instagram requires login" for an error body whose JSON `message` is `checkpoint_required`, `challenge_required` or `login_required`, or whose `<html>` class list contains `not-logged-in`. Verified live against the simulator session (both endpoints above). `USER_ID: "0"` on the homepage is not a signal: it is also `0` for logged-in Instagram-only accounts.
- Native: `webDataRequest`/`webTextRequest` throw `SourceError.notConfigured` for those bodies (`instagramBodyRequiresLogin`), so the account is stored as `invalidCredentials` and Settings shows the reconnect. Covered by `testInstagramDetectsLoginRequiredBodies`; verified on the simulator (account status flipped to `invalidCredentials` on launch).
- The probe also gained `direct-thread`, `direct-seen` and `direct-send-text` commands for the upcoming Instagram chat view; they are not yet validated live because of the checkpoint.
- Follow-up: the login WebView's reconnect seeded the stored (checkpointed) cookies and completed on the homepage, which still renders signed in under a checkpoint, so it re-saved the same dead session. Before accepting cookies it now calls `GET /api/v1/accounts/edit/web_form_data/` from the page; a `checkpoint_required` answer (which carries the challenge URL) or a logged-out page opens the challenge instead. `/challenge/` hands off to Meta's `/auth_platform/logged_in_redirect/` pages, which count as part of the challenge, and the challenge is opened at most once, so a still-locked session is never saved (an earlier version looped between the two). Probe command: `session-check`. Verified on the simulator: the reconnect now shows Instagram's "Check your text messages" code step. The Instagram chat view is deferred; the probe's `direct-thread`, `direct-seen` and `direct-send-text` commands remain unvalidated.

### 2026-10-26 — Shared chat screen; Spotify Messages chat

- The Apron chat UI is now network-agnostic. `ChatViewModel` (Core) holds the published chat state and display models (`ChatMessage`, `ChatUser`, `ChatEmbed`, `ChatReaction`, `ChatReactor`, `ChatAttachment`) with overridable actions and capability flags (`canReply`, `canReact`, `offersAttachments`, `mentionPattern`, `showsSenderNames`); unsupported actions default to no-ops and `connect`/`send` must be overridden. `ApronChatViewModel` subclasses it, keeps its `ApronMessage` snapshots internally and maps them to `ChatMessage` on publish (embeds, including the allowed-media-origin rule, are now mapped in the view model). `ChatUIKitView`/`ChatViewController` (renamed from the Apron controller, with its cells and helpers renamed `Chat*`) take any `ChatViewModel`; cells re-render when their `ChatMessage` or group layout changes rather than on `log_id`. `ChatView` is the SwiftUI screen (title pill, connect/disconnect, reconnect on activation); `ApronChatView` adds members and profiles on top, and `NetworkChatView` hosts the other networks. `AppContainer.chatViewModelFactory(for:)` decides which feed items open a chat.
- Spotify Messages items open `SpotifyChatViewModel`: `speechless/v1/retrieve/hierarchy` pages of 30 (newest first, `next_page_token` for older), a 10-second poll of the newest page while open (Spotify's realtime push channel isn't available), text sending, and reactions shown read-only from child nodes. DM titles use the other member's profile name and hide sender names; group chats use the group name.
- **Send (live-validated):** `POST speechless/v1/submit/request` with `ClientNodeRequest{1: NodeRequest{2: MessageRequest{1: AddContribution{1: chat id, 2: ContributionPayload{1: FreeText{1: text}}}}}}`. Like the Android app, no node id, timestamp or digest is sent; the server assigns them and the `ClientNodeResponse` echoes the created `ClientNode` (`spotify:chat-message:<id>`), which the chat inserts directly. There is no reply reference for text. Schema from a jadx decompile of the Android app (`com.spotify.campfire.datasource.impl.proto`; request builder in the obfuscated repository class). One test message was sent to a chat the user chose with `spotify-web-client.py chat-send`.
- **Seen (live-validated):** `ClientNodeRequest{1: NodeRequest{3: BulkMessageRequest{2: AddHierarchyActivity{1: chat id, 2: ActivityRequest{3: ack_type USER_SEEN=1}}}}}` marks the whole chat seen (`chat-seen`; a read-back showed every message `user_seen`). The chat sends it once per newer incoming message that becomes fully visible.
- **Track metadata:** shared tracks use spclient `GET metadata/4/track/<gid>?market=from_token` with the WebPlayer bearer, as the Spotify apps do; the gid is the base62 id as 32 hex digits. It returns name, artists, album, release date, duration and cover file ids (`https://i.scdn.co/image/<file_id>`), but no preview. Probe: `track-metadata`. Albums, episodes and playlists keep the oEmbed card.
- **Inline previews:** a new `.audio` embed renders a card with artwork and a play/pause button; the clip URL comes from the existing embed-page `audioPreview` (`trackPreviewURL`, probe `track-preview`) through `ChatViewModel.audioPreviewURL(for:)`, cached per track. One `ChatAudioPreviewPlayer` per chat plays one preview at a time under the `.playback` session and stops when the chat disappears; tapping elsewhere on the card opens the track in Spotify.
- Verified with `SpotifyMessagesParserTests` (request bytes match the probe's dry-run output, history/reaction parsing, gid conversion), `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, and on the simulator: the chat opened with track cards, previews played, switched and paused, and the Apron chat still rendered on the shared controller.

### 2026-10-26 — Development profile with iCloud for device installs

- `stupid-app run --network` failed signing: the stored development profile predated the iCloud capability, so it did not authorize `com.apple.developer.ubiquity-kvstore-identifier`. `stupid-app signing setup --kind development` with the default name then failed with HTTP 409 because App Store Connect already holds two profiles named `tech.stupid.StupidSocial Development`. Passing `--profile-name "iCloud"` created `iCloud tech.stupid.StupidSocial Development` with the current capabilities, and the network install then succeeded. The duplicate old profiles are left in place; delete them in App Store Connect if the default name is needed again.

### 2026-10-26 — Chats screen left of the feed

- `ContentView`'s root is now `HomePager`, a `UIPageViewController` with two child `UIHostingController`s: a `NavigationStack` for `ChatsView` on the left and the existing feed stack on the right, opening on the feed. SwiftUI's paging `ScrollView` (`.scrollTargetBehavior(.paging)`) and paging `TabView` were tried first: both left the large navigation titles flush with the screen edge (the hosted navigation bars lost their layout margins), and the `ScrollView`'s initial `scrollPosition` didn't apply. Paging turns off (the page controller's scroll view `isScrollEnabled`) whenever either stack's root has disappeared, i.e. a screen is pushed, so the system back swipe and swipe-to-reply in chats aren't taken over; verified that a right swipe in a chat pushed from the feed pops back instead of paging to Chats.
- `ChatListViewModel` merges `ChatSummary`s from `ApronNotificationSource.chatSummaries()` (every joined room on every server, regardless of age; same `room_list` plus per-room `history` reads as the feed, unread counted with the shared read store) and `SpotifyMessagesSource.chatSummaries()` (`chats-share-set/latest` with limit 50; DMs titled and pictured with the other member's profile; previews prefixed "You: " or, in groups, the sender; unread from `HierarchyStatistic.total_user_unseen_events`, now parsed as `SpotifyChat.unseenCount`). A network that fails keeps its previous rows and shows its error above the list; one that isn't configured contributes nothing. The list reloads on appear, on returning from a chat, and on pull-to-refresh. Rows open `ApronChatView` or the Spotify chat via `AppContainer.spotifyChatFactory`.
- Follow-ups: an animated programmatic `setViewControllers` left `UIPageViewController`'s cached neighbours stale, so swiping back stopped working; it now sets the page again without animation once the animation finishes. The page change also removes the old page's view, firing its root `onDisappear`, which had disabled paging, so paging now depends only on the visible side's root (the hidden side can't have anything pushed, since paging is off while it does). The Chats toolbar button was then removed in favour of `HomePageIndicator`, small dots at the bottom centre, shown only while paging is enabled so they never cover a chat's composer. Chats rows use tighter insets (8/12) and start their separator at the text via `.listRowSeparatorLeading`. A search bar pinned under the Chats title filters by title and latest message with `localizedStandardContains`. It reuses a new shared `searchBar(text:prompt:placement:)` modifier; Search keeps its `.automatic` placement (floating at the bottom on iOS 26) and Chats uses `.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .always)`.
- Page dots now follow the swipe continuously, styled like the story rows' indicator (5 pt dots growing to 7 pt and darkening with closeness to their page). `HomePagerController` wraps the page controller, observes its scroll view's `contentOffset` by KVO (the page controller remains the scroll view's delegate) and updates a UIKit `HomePageDotsView` directly. Pushing the offset through SwiftUI state re-rendered both hosting controllers' root views on every frame, which cancelled the page controller's pan and broke swiping.
- "Is a screen pushed" is now tracked from navigation state (`ChatsView`'s selection, the feed's detail selection and the feed path) instead of each root's `onAppear`/`onDisappear`: the outgoing page's `onDisappear` fires as a swipe begins, which hid the dots and disabled paging mid-drag.
- Chats: no separator above the first row (`.listRowSeparator(.hidden, edges: .top)` on that row only; hiding the top edge on every row also hid the separators shared between rows), and the network badge sits on an opaque system-background tile, since Spotify's round badge has transparent corners. Opening a chat cancels the list's in-flight reload (its `.task` restarts), which surfaced "Spotify: cancelled"; `CancellationError` and `URLError.cancelled` no longer count as failures.
- Chats rows show the time for today, the abbreviated weekday within the past 6 days, numeric day and month earlier this year (`.dateTime.day().month(.defaultDigits)`), and the numeric date with year before that (`.numeric`), all through `Date.FormatStyle`, so the order and separators follow the user's locale (`ChatRow.timeLabel`).

### 2026-10-26 — Feed row insets; last page restored on launch

- Feed rows now use the same insets as Chats rows (8 pt vertical, 12 pt horizontal) through a shared `compactListRowInsets()` modifier, and `NotificationRow` dropped its extra 4 pt vertical padding.
- The home page (Chats or Social) is stored in `@AppStorage("homePage")` (`HomePage` is now `String`-backed), so the app reopens on the side it was left on. Verified by relaunching on each side; the page dots start on the restored side.
- The Chats list is saved to `Caches/chat-list.json` after each load and restored when `ChatListViewModel` is created, so the loading spinner only appears on the very first load; later launches show the saved list while it refreshes. A network that is no longer connected loads as empty and drops out of the saved list. With the search bar in the navigation bar's drawer, rows present while the bar first lays out started the list collapsed to the inline title (confirmed by removing the search bar, which restored the large title; `defaultScrollAnchor(.top, for: .initialOffset)` and inserting rows on the first `.task` pass did not help). `ChatsView` therefore inserts its rows 50 ms after appearing; verified over three relaunches that the large title, search bar and saved chats show without a spinner.

### 2026-10-26 — Chats moved out of the feed

- Apron rooms and Spotify Messages chats now appear only on the Chats screen. `ApronNotificationSource` and `SpotifyMessagesSource` are no longer feed sources (`NotificationFetching` and their feed-item code were removed); they only build `ChatSummary`s, and `ApronNotificationSource` still validates the Apron account. The Apron server-cursor listening during a short-lived read and the fallback for servers without `rooms` (default room learned from history) moved into the chat-summary path.
- `ApronLiveFeed` keeps its connection per server but now rebuilds that server's Chats rows on the open session (`ChatListViewModel.replaceApronChats`) instead of writing feed items to the notification cache, so the Chats list updates live. Removing a server drops its rows the same way.
- The feed lost its Apron/Spotify chat routing, the item-based chat factory, and the Apron unread badge on rows. `FeedService` deletes cached `.apron` and `.spotify` feed items on start, since they'd otherwise linger for the 24-hour retention (Spotify had no other feed items). Instagram DMs stay in the feed until Instagram has a chat screen.
- Verified with `SpotifyMessagesParserTests` and `FeedServiceTests`, `build-for-testing`, and on the simulator (no Apron or Spotify rows in the feed; the Chats list shows all rooms and chats with live Apron updates).

### 2026-10-26 — Chat keyboard dismissal; Chats rows follow the open chat

- Tapping the message list dismisses the composer's keyboard (a tap recognizer with `cancelsTouchesInView = false`, so links, cards, images and reactions still get their taps). Verified on the simulator by the composer's caret disappearing after a tap between bubbles.
- Returning from a chat briefly showed the row's old latest message and unread count until the list reloaded. `ChatListViewModel.follow(_:chatId:)` now wires a chat opened from the list to its row: `ChatViewModel` reports its newest message (skipped while an older window is showing) with a one-line preview (text with mentions by name, "Shared “title”" for cards and tracks, "Photo" for images) and its unread count whenever `messages` or `unreadCount` change, and the list rewrites that row (`You: ` prefix for your messages, the sender's name where `ChatSummary.namesSenderInPreview`, set for Apron rooms and Spotify groups). Spotify reports no unread count, so its row reads 0 once opened, matching the chat being marked seen. The reload on return still runs afterwards. Saved lists from before `namesSenderInPreview` don't decode and are rebuilt by the first load. Not yet verified with a message arriving while a chat is open.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron "Room listing limited"

- Returning to Chats after sending a message in an Apron room showed "Apron: Room listing limited". That is the reference server's `retry_after` for `room_list` (`apron-server-cloudflare`, `handleRoomList`): its free plan allows `roomListRequestsPerUserMinute: 6`, and only the first `filter: "joined"` listing after authentication is exempt. The app listed rooms on every chat open, on every live message rebuild, and on every return to the Chats list.
- `ApronConnection` now keeps the last joined-rooms listing (`joinedRooms()`, always with `members: true`, so the chat and the Chats list share it), coalesces concurrent requests, and drops the listing when a `room_update` arrives (bumping a generation so a listing requested before it isn't kept). Rooms are therefore listed about once per connection and again after rooms or memberships change.
- The server also caps frames per connection (60 a minute on the free plan), so a live `message` now rebuilds only its room's row (`chatSummaries(in:roomIds:)` and `ChatListViewModel.updateChats`); `room_update` and the initial connect still rebuild every room on the server.
- Verified on the simulator by opening an Apron room and returning to Chats eight times within a minute with no error (previously the seventh listing in a minute was refused).

### 2026-10-26 — Smoother story row pager

- The story row pager's wrap-around used to put a copy of the last row before the first and a copy of the first after the last. Landing on a copy fired a jump without animation to the real row, so the pager snapped visibly and sometimes fought the paging deceleration. Now the modes repeat 200 times in a `LazyVStack`, and the pager starts in the middle at a multiple of the mode count. Page `i` shows mode `i % count`, so wrapping around is plain scrolling, with no sentinels and no programmatic jumps. When the set of modes changes, the pager goes back to the middle and keeps the current mode if it still exists.
- The per-frame scroll offset for the dots used to live in `StoriesBar`'s `@State`. Each frame then rebuilt every row, filtering and sorting the items once per page. The offset now lives in a small `@Observable` object that only `StoryPagerDots` reads, so scrolling re-renders only the dots. Rows are filtered and sorted once per body evaluation.

### 2026-10-26 — Smoother reaction animation

- Reacting from a message's long-press menu used to update the cell while the menu's dismissal preview was still animating. The preview's visible path had the old bubble size, so the grown bubble showed cut in half, and then a separate scroll moved the list up. Reaction changes made while a context menu is showing now wait until its dismissal animation finishes (`willDisplayContextMenu` / `willEndContextMenuInteraction`). Reaction updates then grow the cell and follow the bottom in one 0.3 s animation (`applyKeepingBottom(_:animated:)`). Other growth (quotes, mention names) still applies without animation.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron private room titles, last seen, quotes

- Protocol v9 (shazow/apron `dc137a1`) adds `seen` to user objects: the time the shown status last became `offline`, in `log_id` format (milliseconds), sent only with a shown `offline`. A server that sends it must include it in complete objects, such as `room_list` `users`, and in each `user` notification that changes the shown status to `offline`. Clients show it only while the shown status is `offline`.
- The demo server (`apron-cloudflare-demo/9`) doesn't send `seen` yet. `scripts/apron-web-client.py rooms wss://server.apron.chat/ --members -v`, signed in as an account, lists offline users in `users` with `status: "offline"` and no `seen`. This replaces the earlier note that Apron has no last-seen time.
- `ApronUser` now decodes, merges, and caches `seen`. `lastSeen` returns it as a date only while `status` is `offline`. The profile and offline member rows show "Last seen 5 minutes ago". You never see your own. This hasn't been seen against a live server yet.
- Private rooms: the demo server lists a DM as `{"room_id": "private_…", "private": true, "members": [{"user_id": …}, …]}` with no title, and members carry only `user_id`; names come from the listing's `users`. `ApronRoom` now decodes `private`. `displayTitle(users:you:)` follows apron-web (`untitledPrivate` and `membersTitle` in `src/lib/protocol/client.ts`, `peopleTitle` in `client-views.ts`). An untitled top-level private room takes the names of its other members, sorted and joined with ", ". It reads "You" when no one else is left, and "Private room" without a member list. Other rooms keep the title or `room_id`. The Chats list and the chat title use it, and a room with one other member shows that person's avatar in the Chats row, as apron-web does.
- Quotes: messages are parsed as inline Markdown, so `>` lines used to show the marker literally. For Markdown messages, `ChatAttributedText.formatQuotes` drops the `>` marker from each quoted line, indents the line by 12 pt, dims plain text to 70% opacity, and tags it `.chatQuote`. `ChatLinkLabel` draws a 3 pt bar beside each run of quoted lines. The bars are sublayers placed in `layoutSubviews`, because UILabel on iOS 27 rarely calls `drawText(in:)`. Plain-text messages (Instagram, Spotify) are unchanged.
- `ChatLinkLabel.layout(in:)` is now shared by the bars and tap hit-testing. It returns the `NSTextStorage` too, since `NSLayoutManager` holds its storage weakly. Without it the layout was empty and no bars appeared.
- Verified with the new `ApronModelsTests` (DM shape from the demo server, title fallbacks, `seen` only while offline), `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build`, and on the simulator: the DM shows as "shazow, vrypan" in Chats and in the chat title, and a quoted message shows the bar and indent.

### 2026-10-26 — Steady opening at unread messages; redacted names in chats

- Reproduced on the local js-minimal server (`bun third-party/apron/servers/js-minimal/apron-server.js`) with images served from `127.0.0.1:8770`. Each round seeded 40 unread messages as another guest: text, sized and unsized uploads, link previews with and without an image. Opening the room was recorded with `simctl io recordVideo` and checked frame by frame. `scripts/apron-web-client.py send` gained `--link-url`, `--link-title`, `--link-description` and `--link-image` for link-preview embeds. `--image-size none` sends an `og.image` without a size.
- Cause 1: the room opened at the bottom of the cache. When history brought the unread messages, `applySnapshot` saw the list pinned to the bottom and started an animated scroll there. The divider was handled one run-loop turn later through `$firstUnreadMessageId`. The animation's completion then called `scrollToBottom`, which snapped past the divider to the newest message. Now `applySnapshot` anchors to a divider that appears in that snapshot (unless the user has scrolled), and the `$firstUnreadMessageId` subscription is gone. The completion settles at the bottom only while the list still follows it.
- Cause 2: when the Chats row has unread messages but every cached message is read, the unread ones are all newer than the cache, so showing the cache meant opening at its bottom and then jumping to the divider. `ApronChatViewModel(expectsUnread:)`, set from `ChatSummary.unreadCount > 0`, now holds the cache back. History publishes it together with the new messages, and the room opens straight at the divider. If history hasn't arrived after 1.5 s, the cache is shown anyway.
- Cause 3: with unread messages already in the cache, the room opened at the cache's divider, then moved to a later one. `dropCacheBehindGap` dropped every cached message older than a newest page with `more`, even when the cache reached into that page and there was no gap, and the divider went with them. It now drops the cache only when its newest message is older than the page. Also, cached messages on screen were marked read before the connection was up, which moved the cursor and so the re-placed divider. `reportVisibleRead` now waits for `.connected` and runs once on connecting.
- Verified on the simulator: with a fully read cache it opens at "New Messages" with no movement as images load; with unread messages in the cache it opens at the cache's divider and stays; scrolling still advances the cursor; jump to latest clears the Chats badge; a read room opens at the bottom.
- Redact names now covers the chat screen. `DebugRedaction.isEnabled` reads the same `redactionEnabled` default for UIKit code. Sender names, avatar initials, reply quotes and the reply preview, typing names, reactors, mention suggestions, and mentions in text (shown as `@Redacted`) are redacted, along with the Apron member list and profile (name and `@user_id`). The title is redacted when it names people: one-to-one chats (`!showsSenderNames`) and Apron rooms named for their members (`ChatViewModel.titleNamesPeople`). An `@username` subtitle is redacted too. Names written as plain text in a message body are not redacted. The Chats list is unchanged.
- Link and file cards: the symbol hugged its content but had no compression resistance, so a long title (which truncates in the middle) squeezed the `link` symbol to a sliver. Symbols now have a fixed 22 pt frame with required compression resistance.
- Embeds are always full width. A bubble with link previews or cards takes the maximum content width (`maxContentWidth`), and `embedsStack` fills it (`.fill`, was `.leading`). Preview images drop their fixed 240 pt width and keep only the aspect ratio, cropped to at most 4:3 portrait (`max(ratio, 0.75)`). Uploaded images in the media section keep their Telegram-style sizes.

### 2026-10-26 — Apron servers sync through iCloud

- Before: Apron tokens already synced through the iCloud Keychain (`saveApronToken` uses the synchronizable store), and room read cursors through iCloud Key-Value storage. The server list (`account.apron.servers`) was in local `UserDefaults` only, so another device had the tokens but no servers.
- `ApronServerSync` keeps one entry per server URL in iCloud KVS (`com.stupid.apron.servers`, mirrored locally as `apronServerSync`): the requested name, whether it was removed, and when it last changed. Removed servers stay as entries, so a device that missed a removal can't bring the server back. Local and remote entries merge per server, with the newer change winning, and the result is written to both sides, as `StorySeenSync` does.
- `ApronService` applies the entries at launch and on `didChangeExternallyNotification`. It adds servers saved on other devices and drops ones removed there. Servers saved before this change are recorded on first launch, so they reach the other devices. A synced server is added with no user and signs in on first use, through the live feed or Chats, with the token from the iCloud Keychain when there is one. Adding a server, or a sign-in with a new requested name, records an add; removing one records a removal. A removal from another device drops this device's cursors, cache and drafts, but leaves the token to the Keychain's own sync. Server changes post `serversDidChangeNotification`. `ApronServersViewModel` reloads on it, and `ApronLiveFeed` disconnects removed servers and clears their Chats rows.
- Loopback servers (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`), such as the local js-minimal test server, aren't synced: on another device they would point at that device.
- Own user IDs (`apronOwnUserIds`) now also sync through KVS (`com.stupid.apron.ownusers`) as a per-server union. Messages sent as a guest from another device then render as your own.
- Verified with `ApronModelsTests` (merge: the newer change wins, removals win over older adds; loopback stays local), `stupid-app build`, and on the simulator: the existing demo server was recorded, the local server wasn't, and both stayed saved. Syncing between two devices hasn't been tested yet; it needs two devices signed into the same iCloud account.

### 2026-10-27 — Apron links

- Apron links (PROTOCOL.md Appendix A.4, link.apron.chat `SPEC.md`) open in the app. `ApronLink` parses both forms, `https://link.apron.chat/#…` and `apron:?…`. Keys are form-decoded (a bare `+` is a space) and the first value of a repeated key wins. Unknown keys are ignored. For the web form, keys in the query count after the fragment's. A `token` in the query is refused, because link.apron.chat has already received it. `server` must be `wss`, or `ws` on a loopback host, with no user info or fragment. An empty path becomes `/` so links match saved servers. Other transports, such as `webrtc`, fail with an error that names the transport. `room`/`user` must be non-empty and contain no whitespace or control characters. `message` must be a positive decimal below 2^53 with no leading zero, and needs `room`. A link without `server` is only valid in content from an Apron server: it resolves against that chat's server and is refused anywhere else. `webURL` writes the web form, with SPEC.md §4 encoding and never a token. Covered by `ApronLinkTests`.
- Entry points: the `apron` URL scheme (Info.plist, handled by `ContentView.onOpenURL`) and taps in chat text. Bare `apron:?…` links are found with a regex, since `NSDataDetector` doesn't match them. Link cards and image embeds go through the same route. `NotificationRouter.open(_:contentServer:)` is the single entry point. A link to a room on a saved server that is already in the Chats list opens directly. Anything else shows `ApronLinkView` (`ApronLinkViewModel`), which leads with the server's host in ASCII (punycode) form, shows IDs with hidden and reordering characters escaped (in the regular font), and does nothing until the user taps. The sheet has these actions: add an unknown server as a guest (with a stronger warning for loopback/private hosts); join an unjoined room (opening the room joins it); sign in with a link's token (`scheme: "token"`, via `addServer`/`updateToken`); or just show a saved server. The app never makes email/external proposals, so every sign-in link counts as one "without the state of its own proposal" and needs confirmation.
- A linked message opens the room scrolled to it: `ApronChatViewModel.focusMessageId` runs `jump(to:)` after the first load. A linked member opens their profile (`ApronChatView(profileUserId:)`). A link to the open room jumps or shows the profile in place. The focus travels as `NotificationRouter.pendingFocus` next to `pendingChat`, so `ChatSummary.Destination` and its cached encoding didn't change.
- Apron messages have a "Copy Link" menu item that copies the web form. Notices without a `message_id` don't get one.
- Not done: universal links for `https://link.apron.chat`. These need the app in link.apron.chat's `clients.json` registry, which generates the AASA file, plus an `applinks:link.apron.chat` associated-domains entitlement. Until then, those links open in the app only when tapped inside it; from elsewhere they open the link.apron.chat page in Safari.
- Verified on the simulator against the local server (`scripts/apron-local-server.sh`, messages posted as `alice` with the probe): adding a server from a link with a message, which opened the room scrolled to the message; a joined room opening directly; a same-room message link, a member link and a cross-server web link in chat text; and the webrtc error. The Join Room and Sign In sheets were shown but not confirmed.

### 2026-10-28 — X Chat (encrypted X DMs)

- Investigation: legacy DMs still answer (`GET /i/api/1.1/dm/inbox_initial_state.json`, `dm/conversation/<id>.json` paged with `max_id`, `dm/user_updates.json?cursor=`, and `GET /i/api/2/badge_count/badge_count.json`; probe commands `dm-inbox`, `dm-conversation`, `dm-updates`, `dm-unread-count` in `scripts/x-web-client.py`, which now also reads credentials with `--env-local` from `DIAGNOSE_X_CREDS`). But the newest non-request legacy conversation was months old: conversations moved to X Chat, which x.com loads as a module-federated app (`chat.x.com/embed-v1.json`, assets under `abs.twimg.com/x-web/xchat/embed/`, protocol in the Kotlin/JS `@x-clients/dm-core`). The bundle was mapped and every claim below validated live with the new `scripts/xchat-client.py` before porting.
- Validated live (probe, read-only unless noted): Juicebox recovery with the user's PIN through the official JS SDK (`juicebox-sdk` 0.3.7 under bun; the bundler build needs the wasm instantiated by hand) returned a 64-byte secret whose halves derive the registered identity and signing public keys. 9 of 9 conversation keys in the first inbox page unwrapped; all 323 message events with a key opened with a valid MAC (contents version 0 uses the conversation key directly; the bundle's per-message HKDF applies only to version 1, which no event used). All 323 create and 139 read-marker signatures verified against the canonical payloads (the create payload covers the base64 ciphertext, not the plaintext, and the base64 is unpadded). 280 of 291 franking tags matched; the 11 that didn't carry media hashes, which need their own decryption. Inbox paging returned 34 conversations; computed unread counts first disagreed with X's `xchat_unread_count` (4 vs 0) because incoming reactions are create events, and matched once only decrypted messages counted. Mutations, in a conversation the user named for testing: a text message, a reaction and a read marker were accepted, read back, and their signatures and franking verified like X's own.
- `GetConversationPageQuery`'s `min_local_sequence_id` is an exclusive upper bound: `0` fails with "range order reversed; begin must be <= end", so the newest page uses `Int64.max`.
- Swift: `XChatThrift` (generic binary-protocol coder), `XChatCrypto` (CryptoKit P-256/AES-GCM/SHA-256; XSalsa20-Poly1305 written natively, as Telegram's AES-IGE is, and checked against PyNaCl vectors at Poly1305 block boundaries; CryptoKit accepts the 16-byte GCM IV), `XChatClient` (actor; its own `KeychainCredentialStore`, as `TelegramClient` has), `XChatViewModel`, `XChatUnlockViewModel` and the Settings → X section. `XClient.apiHeaders` is now a shared nonisolated helper. X rows join the Chats list and archived X chats its Archived section.
- Juicebox in Swift: `Vendor/JuiceboxSdk` holds the SDK's Swift wrapper and an XCFramework of its prebuilt FFI static libraries, made by `scripts/vendor-juicebox.sh` (checksums verified, stripped from 58 MB to about 5 MB per slice). stupid-app rejected all binary targets, so stupid-app 0.0.22 was released to link local static-library XCFrameworks (validated by its planner against the XCFramework's `Info.plist`).
- SwiftFormat's defaults drop `Sendable` from internal types, which this module's main-actor default isolation then treats as non-Sendable across actors; the X Chat value types that cross the actor boundary are public, like Telegram's.
- Decisions (polling was replaced by the WebSocket the same day, see below): group chats named for members since titles are encrypted group events; sending replies off until the reply preview's re-encrypted source event is validated; encrypted media shown as a card that opens X; undecryptable messages shown, not hidden. The keys are synchronizable like the X cookies, so one PIN entry covers the user's devices.
- Verified: `XChatCryptoTests` (7 tests: secretbox vectors, tampering, ECIES unwrap, franking, signatures, Thrift round trip), `swiftformat`, `stupid-app build` (device) and `stupid-app run --simulator` with stupid-app 0.0.22. On the simulator: Settings → X → PIN unlocked the keys, the X chat rows appeared, the test conversation opened with its messages and reaction decrypted, and a message sent from the app was read back by the probe with a valid signature and franking tag.
- Not done: legacy (pre-X Chat) conversations, message requests, encrypted media, sending replies and media, group titles, and push.

### 2026-10-28 — X Chat over the WebSocket

- Validated with the new `scripts/xchat-client.py listen` (uses `websockets` through uv): `GenerateXChatTokenMutation` mints a token, `wss://chat-ws.x.com/ws?token=` accepts it with `Origin: https://x.com` and no subprotocol, the server sends nothing on open, and the 30 s keep-alive (`Message{2: MessageInstruction{2: KeepAliveInstruction{}}}`, from dm-core's heartbeat cron) keeps it open without a reply. With `--send-to`, a message sent through GraphQL during the listen came back on the socket about 0.1 s later as `Message{1: MessageEvent}` with the same sequence id.
- `XChatLiveFeed` (one socket, foreground only, like `ApronLiveFeed`) replaces the chat's 15 s polling: pushed events go through `XChatClient.ingest` to the Chats rows and to open chats; reconnects and pull instructions catch up with the newest page and a list reload. `XChatConversationInfo`'s event lists are now `var` for this. Unlocking or locking X Chat restarts the feed (`XChatUnlockViewModel.refresh` now reports a change, so disconnecting X closes it too).
- Verified on the simulator with messages sent by the probe to the test conversation: the Chats row's preview and position changed within 2 s with the list open, a message appeared within 2 s in the open chat, and one sent while the app was in the background was there after returning (catch-up on reconnect).
- Reactions render with their messages. The chat screen applies `messages` and `reactions` from separate subscriptions, and a cell takes its reactions from what the screen already holds. Telegram, Instagram and Spotify set `history` before `reactions`, so new bubbles rendered bare and grew when the reactions arrived. `ChatViewModel.publish(history:reactions:)` sets reactions first; the screen keeps totals for messages it hasn't shown yet, so the first render has them. Apron already applied reactions before publishing messages (also from its message cache). Verified by capturing frames while a Telegram chat with reactions opened: the reactions were there in the first frame with messages. The in-progress XChat view model isn't changed yet and should call the same method.
- Opening a Telegram, Instagram or Spotify chat with unread messages no longer jumps. Each opened with the snapshot from the last visit, then history added the new messages and the "New Messages" divider, so the list grew and scrolled to the divider. `ChatsView` now calls `ChatViewModel.expectUnread()` when the chat's row has unread messages. If the restored messages hold none of them (`unreadCount == 0`), `messages` stays empty until `state` leaves `.connecting` (history arrived, or failed) or 1.5 seconds pass, as Apron already does with `expectsUnread`. The chat then opens once, at the divider. Telegram and Instagram count unread from `history` so the count stays right while messages are held. They also set `firstUnreadMessageId` from the restored snapshot before its first publish, so a snapshot that does hold the unread opens at the divider too. Spotify has no read cursor, so an unread Spotify chat always waits for history. Covered by `ChatViewModelTests`; no chat had unread messages to try live. The in-progress XChat chat should go through `opened(_:chat:)` in `ChatsView` too.
